# STOCK ANALYSIS STRATEGY — Decision Engine (Peaceful Investing / Vijay Malik method)

> **READ THIS FIRST — what this file is.**
> This is my (Claude's) operating manual for deciding whether to BUY, HOLD, AVOID, or SELL a stock.
> It is a *mechanical decision system*, not an essay. I do **not** need to reason from first principles or
> "use my brain" on philosophy — I apply the rules, thresholds, and worked examples below and reach a verdict.
> Every parameter has: (a) an exact numeric threshold, (b) the formula, (c) a "GOOD looks like / BAD looks like"
> worked example with real numbers so I can pattern-match fast, and (d) what the verdict contribution is.
>
> **Philosophy deliberately omitted.** Arguments for long-term vs trading, "stocks are businesses," motivational
> content, why-fundamental-beats-technical, marketing of paid services — all skipped. Only decision-relevant content is here.
>
> **Pipeline split (multi-agent):** Part 1 — SCREENING lives in `screening.md` (a separate agent writes shortlists to
> `To-Analyze/`). Part 2 — COLLECTION lives in `collection.md` (a separate agent downloads every company's documents +
> data into `To-Analyze/<TICKER>/` with a manifest.json ledger). This file starts where those end (§1 = intake from the
> collected folders). Do NOT re-run screening or re-download documents from here — consume what the manifest says exists.
>
> **Context of the source:** Indian equities (BSE/NSE), bottom-up fundamental value investing, retail/long-term horizon,
> data source = screener.in. Currency ₹. "cr" = crore = 10 million. "lakh" = 0.1 million. Numbers in examples are circa 2014–2020.
> Interest-rate / G-Sec numbers must be refreshed to current values at decision time (see §5).
>
> **Built from fourteen sources:** (1) the *Peaceful Investing* method book (framework — §1–§16); (2) the *Case Studies* ebook
> (20 worked verdicts — **§17**); (3) the *Company Analyses Vol. 1* ebook (11 forensic deep-dives — **§18**, forensic red-flag catalog);
> (4) the *Company Analyses Vol. 2* ebook (13 more deep-dives — **§19**, with a valuation/accounting-manipulation catalog);
> (5) the *Company Analyses Vol. 3* ebook (10 deep-dives — **§20**, the **margin-source triage** book: *decompose every OPM/margin gain into
> real pricing power vs cost-efficiency vs external/temporary luck*);
> (6) the *Company Analyses Vol. 4* ebook (10 deep-dives — **§21**: **the business-return / benchmarking-formula book** — *does the asset earn more
> than an FD? is the pass-through a fixed-% or a fixed-₹ margin? and the sector/ownership vetoes: EPC, PSU, MNC-parent, promoter loan-back*);
> (7) the *Company Analyses Vol. 5* ebook (10 deep-dives — **§22**: **the cyclical-&-value-trap book** — *never value a cyclical or a weak business on
> peak/trailing P/E; a low P/E is usually a trap; a brand/software is only a moat if it earns durable superior margins; and even great optics lose to a governance veto*);
> (8) the *Company Analyses Vol. 6* ebook (9 deep-dives — **§23**: **the capital-allocation & group-ATM book** — *score every company on "value created per ₹ retained";
> IT-services margin is a currency bet not pricing power; a customer-captive supplier is a permanent price-taker; and the deadliest veto is a listco used as the promoter group's cash ATM — ICDs, promoter-owned suppliers, sell-through-promoter firms, asymmetric JV valuations*);
> (9) the *Company Analyses Vol. 7* ebook (8 deep-dives — **§24**: **the cyclical-capital-trap & conglomerate-shuffle book** — *a capital-intensive + low-margin business is a structural debt trap (low NFAT × low NPM → SSGR << growth → debt-funded → value destroyed, e.g. Rain "₹0.31 created per ₹1 retained"); cCFO >> cPAT is BENIGN when it's just interest + depreciation add-back in a levered firm; and the group treats the listco as one pocket — pushing junk IN (Hinduja Foundries), hiving good divisions OUT to sister cos without bidding (Century → UltraTech/Grasim), warrant games, "throwing good money after bad," and net-cash that still needed a promoter bailout*);
> (10) the *Company Analyses Vol. 8* ebook (9 deep-dives — **§25**: **the good-business-no-margin-of-safety & anti-moat-archetype book** — *even a genuinely good business (Supreme, Atul, AIA) is an AVOID at P/E 31–42 with unaddressed blemishes; learn the four structural anti-moats by sight — contract-manufacturer-for-big-brands (Amber: customers hold all power → NPM 1–3%), platform-disruption (Just Dial: Google ate the moat → **−₹7.64 created per ₹1 retained**, P/E 65→7 while PAT doubled = the HIGH-P/E de-rating trap), temporary-external-supply-windfall (IOL: BASF-shutdown OPM 9→30% already reverting), and auto-ancillary-scale-without-self-funding (Minda: FCF −₹837cr, serial dilution); pricing power is REAL only when RM%-of-sales falls (Atul 59→48%), otherwise it's conditional/lagged pass-through (Supreme, AIA, Amber); and cCFO<cPAT can be BENIGN when it's a deliberate WC-intensive moat (AIA holds inventory next to the customer)*);
> and (11) the *Company Analyses Vol. 9* ebook (10 deep-dives — **§26**: **the governance-density & cash-flow-forensics book** — *all 10 names carried material promoter/controls flags; REBUILD every cash-flow statement before trusting CFO (borrowings & finance costs hidden in CFI at NGL, short-term borrowings inside CFO at Beekay +₹18cr, share/MF investments inside CFO); new extraction mechanics — equity-raised-then-lent-out-as-ICDs (Lincoln), promoter companies merged INTO the listco at P/E 35–151 (Valiant/Aarti, Lincoln-LPRL), same-brand promoter competitors (SOM's SDPL sells the listco's own IMFL brands; Monte Carlo's domain owned by the promoter co), interest-free promoter current-accounts funded by fresh listco debt (SOM), capex-that-created-no-capacity (SOM ₹23.4cr), disappearing related parties, outsized "sales promotion + advances to retailers" vs peers (SOM 11–15% of revenue vs 0.2–6%); formula-based/lagged pass-through is NOT pricing power (Deepak quarterly resets; Jamna's contractual "provision for price differences" clawback); supply-disruption windfalls revert ~90% in a year (Deepak DASDA EBIT 55%→7%); and the converter/fixed-conversion-fee model gives a commodity player stabler margins (Beekay 13% vs SAIL/Tata ~0% at the FY16 trough)*);
> and (12) the *Company Analyses Vol. 10* ebook (10 deep-dives — **§27**: **the structural-mitigants-vs-structural-caps & owner-extraction book** — *engineered margin-defense mechanics are cushions, NOT moats — score them one notch (Polycab's ≤90-day price-list resets + channel financing that shifts dealer credit risk to banks; Laurus's customer-funded dedicated plants + constraint-step capex; Tata Elxsi's rate discipline; converter fees §2[V9]); structural CAPS overrule operator quality — the regulated-sin cage (Godfrey: licensing freeze + FDI ban enforce the oligopoly while ~50%+ taxes cap growth at 3%/yr → value-per-₹-RE 3.96 despite FCF ₹2,051cr), regulator-rented moats (Sharda: the registration queue IS the franchise — stroke-of-a-pen risk, ₹30–50cr/yr intangible write-offs forever), donor-market price erosion (Laurus ARV), dumping/duty cycles on can't-idle furnaces (Asahi); FCFE is the shareholder-truth: Asahi FCF +₹1,212cr but FCFE −₹237cr (interest ate the decade), Laurus FCFE −₹1,938cr, Safari −₹166cr; OWNER IDENTITY is an analytic input — PE owners milk (Accelya: R&D 10%→0.2% of sales, headcount −20%, payouts 124–185%), MNC parents extract (AGC sold its sick co INTO Asahi then recovered ₹225cr of loans), family dynasties feud through regulators (Modis), operators build (Jatia: Safari 4.7%→27.5% CAGR); and the quality-half was unbuyable (Elxsi P/E ~88–100, Polycab ~48, RHI ~42, Asahi ~35, Laurus ~33) while the cheap-half was correctly-priced caps (Godfrey ~17, Sharda ~19, Accelya ~20) — both P/E tails, twelfth confirmation*);
> and (13) the *Company Analyses Vol. 11* ebook (10 deep-dives — **§28**: **the pedigree-vs-cash-register & SME-pump-and-dump book** — *famous names and MNC pedigree do NOT survive the pricing-power test: Relaxo at P/E 114 with falling volumes and 8–12%-of-sales advertising that buys shelf space but zero hike-tolerance (customers fled a price rise, forcing a 15–20% cut; OPM 21→12%); P&G Hygiene's growth-or-margin THROTTLE (A&P 8→18% of sales = 4× sales but NPM 23→12; cut A&P → margin up, growth zero) plus the four-entity structure that migrates brands to the unlisted arm and a royalty ramp 1.8%→5.4%; Linde's FY2019 "margin re-rating" is an IndAS-115 accounting artifact (pass-through power netted out of BOTH revenue and cost) atop 37 years in which exceptional gains were 64% of cumulative PAT-before-exceptionals — and the parent's RPT web drew a 93.95% shareholder vote-down, a SEBI probe, and the company suing to stop the probe; the value-per-₹-RE spectrum completed: PGHH 216.80 (retention-starved extreme), Linde 26.29 (re-rating artifact), Abbott 23.51 (genuine-ish), Relaxo 15.11 (vs P/E 114!), Rushil 3.45, DRL 2.05 (acquisition-write-off drag on the largest RE base yet), Seshasayee 1.35 (cycle), Ramco 0.67 (FAILS ≥1 — the cross-holding discount priced), Websol −24.03 (negative-RE + hope re-rating); the Varanium Cloud SEBI order = the complete SME-IPO fraud checklist (threshold-dodged ₹40cr issue, 100% IPO-funded projects, ~60% of proceeds siphoned in 2 quarters, fake Seychelles customers, PAT ₹178cr vs CFO ₹8cr, promoter rap sheet googleable, pledge 0→64%, pump announcements then a lock-in-expiry dump of the entire free stake); plus the named cash-flow tricks: CMLTD-inside-CFO (Ramco ₹41cr, Rushil ₹21.5cr), write-back-earnings (Websol: creditors' losses AS the profit history), and cross-holding stake-inflation math (Ramco: promoters' ₹1 controls ₹2 of group stake)*).
> and (14) the *Company Analyses Vol. 12* ebook (10 deep-dives — **§29**: **the story-stock harvest & bull-market-promoter-playbook book** — *how promoters monetize a bull market end-to-end: buy a listed SHELL for pennies, switch the object clause to the season's theme and rename (Nibe: control of what became a ₹2,650cr Mcap assembled for ₹3.48cr; the "defence" company had gross block ₹21 LAKH and 57% traded-goods revenue; TechNvision's lineage: Ankur Agencies → Solix → TechNvision); run an ANNOUNCEMENT MACHINE (MoUs, tie-ups, Gartner/IDC badges, conference-registration PRs filed as corporate announcements — Urja Global's fake "Zacobite" mineral is the SEBI-proven precedent) while the price runs 50× (₹40→₹2,000); time WARRANTS to the run (Nibe allotted at ~₹500, exercised near ₹2,000; KPI's serial warrants; the 25/75 warrant = a FREE promoter option — pay 25%, exercise only if price > strike, else lapse and re-issue lower — if the infusion were genuine it would be a 100% upfront preferential at market, and watch the pledge→warrant-subscription loop, Captain 15.2% pledged); SELL INTO the announcement rally (RIR/Mehta family: ₹29cr sold at ₹835–2,587 since the Odisha-fab news, the independent director sold ALL his shares; Tembo's celebrity independent director resigned by hand-written letter as the stock ran); and EXTRACT through the structure — promoter-land flywheel + royalty rents (KPI: "solar" revenue that was SALE OF PLOT, ₹50cr land advances to the CMD, ₹20.4cr/yr royalty to him for a name he owns), WOS carve-outs and step-down stake dilution (Tembo took the just-created WOS personally; TechNvision's Solix/Emagia diluted to 58–63% with owners undisclosed), the Mehta RPT playbook (sell the listco a defective plant, take the bankrupted co back, then sell it ₹42cr of UNDEVELOPED IP valued by replacement-cost fiction), promoter goodwill buyouts (Wonder: ₹23.42cr in preference shares for ₹8.24cr of net assets from the promoters' own firm), mutual cross-holdings for vote inflation (Captain ↔ Captain Pipes), and the listco as the holding-co's bridge financier (TechNvision borrowed ₹14cr to hand Tiebeam ₹23cr, "honouring group obligations"); the tells that catch it mechanically: value-per-₹-RE BLOWN UP by re-rating on a tiny RE base (Nibe 141.17, RIR 97.04 — the two most manipulated names printed the HIGHEST ratios), FCFE deeply negative across the whole volume (KPI −₹1,148cr, Nibe −₹175cr, Wonder −₹93cr, Dynamic −₹97cr, Captain −₹86cr, Tembo −₹72cr, Advait −₹45cr), SSGR's two BLIND SPOTS exposed (working-capital consumption: Dynamic grew WITHIN its 25% SSGR yet needed debt + ₹96.6cr preferential because receivables/inventory ate ₹294cr; and high-NFAT trading distortion: NFAT >10–20 = trading operations, SSGR not meaningful — Captain), payables-as-unpaid-salaries (TechNvision: payables doubled while the only growing cost was wages), deferred-tax-credit PAT inflation (RIR, Advait), bank-stock-statement vs books divergence (Tembo Q4: ₹28.57cr to the bank vs ₹44cr in books), incomplete ARs filed on exchanges, and the AR that contradicts ITSELF (Captain: "no penalties" in one section, the BSE fine admitted in another); the two honest-but-capped contrasts: Dynamic Cables (real execution, govt-mix inverted 80:20→20:80, HT-cable premiumization — still FCFE-negative with revolving 16.85% promoter loans) and Triveni Engineering (the only kill-test-#1 passer at 2.92: genuine diversified survivor, buybacks ₹965cr, debt flat across a decade — but sugar is a GOVT-ADMINISTERED value chain end-to-end: cane price, sale quotas, levy, buffer stocks, export/import switches, ethanol feedstock caps, even a 40% overnight power-tariff cut, and the promoter family block-bought 10% of crown-jewel Triveni Turbine FROM the listco at the prior close to dodge market-impact cost)*).
> §17–§29 are my fast pattern-match reference: when analyzing any company, find the closest case and copy its reasoning.
> Refinements discovered in the case books are folded into §1–§16 and tagged **[CS]** (Case Studies), **[V1]** (Vol 1), **[V2]** (Vol 2), **[V3]** (Vol 3), **[V4]** (Vol 4), **[V5]** (Vol 5), **[V6]** (Vol 6), **[V7]** (Vol 7), **[V8]** (Vol 8), **[V9]** (Vol 9), **[V10]** (Vol 10), **[V11]** (Vol 11), or **[V12]** (Vol 12).
>
> **Always, before computing anything:** (a) use **CONSOLIDATED** financials, not standalone — standalone hides subsidiary debt
> (MRF standalone missed ₹400cr of subsidiary debt, distorting D/E and interest coverage). (b) **Normalize fiscal-year changes** —
> if a company reports an 18-month (or 9-month) period to align to Apr–Mar, convert to 12 months (×2/3 etc.) before computing growth
> and turnover ratios, else growth/turnover look inflated and receivable-days look low (MRF 2016 = 18 months). (c) Pull **10 years**.

---

## 0. THE ONE-PAGE VERDICT ALGORITHM (run this top to bottom)

A stock is a **BUY** only if it passes **ALL** of: Financial gate, Fraud gate, Business/Moat gate, Management gate, Valuation/MoS gate.
Failing **any single gate = REJECT** (management and fraud gates are hard vetoes — no amount of cheapness compensates).

```
STEP 0  ARCHETYPE TRIAGE (pattern match FIRST) → §17a + the archetype maps atop §18–§29
        match the company to its nearest archetype/case, read that card, carry its
        default verdict + known failure modes into every step below
STEP 1  INTAKE (shortlist arrives from the SCREENING AGENT — see screening.md) → §1
STEP 2  FINANCIAL ANALYSIS (8 params) → §2   gate: must pass ≥ all core thresholds
STEP 3  FRAUD / SHENANIGAN SCAN       → §3   gate: any hard red flag = REJECT
STEP 4  SSGR (inherent growth)        → §4   informs moat + premium
STEP 5  VALUATION + INVESTABLE P/E    → §5   gate: price ≤ target P/E (Margin of Safety in price)
STEP 6  BUSINESS / MOAT (5 tests)     → §6   gate: must show a moat
STEP 7  MANAGEMENT (10 checks)        → §7   gate: HARD VETO — any integrity red flag = REJECT
STEP 8  MARGIN OF SAFETY (3 pillars)  → §8   gate: MoS in price AND business
STEP 9  CREDIT RATING                 → §9   gate: ≥ BBB- and improving trend
STEP 10 OPERATING PERFORMANCE (5)     → §10  confirms trend is intact
STEP 11 FINAL CHECKLIST               → §11  the consolidated pass/fail table
→ BUY SIZING & PORTFOLIO              → §12
→ MONITOR                            → §13
→ SELL RULES                          → §14
WORKED-EXAMPLE LIBRARY (pattern match)→ §15
FORMULA APPENDIX                      → §16
PATTERN-MATCH LIBRARIES (140 cases)   → §17–§29  (find the closest case, copy its reasoning)
FORENSIC RED-FLAG CATALOG (278 items) → §18c → §19c → §20c → §21c → §22c → §23b → §24b → §25b → §26b → §27b → §28b → §29b
MASTER CASE INDEX (alphabetical)      → §29d   |   MASTER value-per-₹-RE TABLE → §5   |   60-SECOND GLANCE → §1
EXCEL-TEMPLATE PROTOCOL (build the dashboard for EVERY company; file: DrVijayMalik_Screener_Excel_Template_v3.2.xlsx) → §16b
```

**Master mental model of an ideal buy (memorize this archetype):**
> A **small/mid-cap**, **pure-play**, **debt-free** company, growing **sales >15% for 10 yrs**, with **NPM >8%**,
> growing **profits ≥ as fast as sales**, **converting profits to cash** (cPAT≈cCFO), generating **positive Free Cash Flow**,
> whose **SSGR > its sales growth** (so growth is self-funded), run by an **honest, minority-friendly promoter** holding **>50%**
> and **not** extracting via salary/related-party/warrants — and available at **P/E < 10** (EY > G-Sec yield).
> Buy it, hold for decades, buy MORE when price falls and fundamentals are intact. **Never sell a good business on price.**

**Hit-rate expectation:** rejection rate is *supposed* to be very high. Finding ~1 good stock/year is plenty.
"10 good stocks in a lifetime can make one a billionaire." Never relax criteria to force a buy. When in doubt → REJECT.

### 0a. The SIXTEEN cross-volume KILL-TESTS (compute ALL of these in every analysis — each one, alone, has rejected companies)

> These are the quantified tests the case-book sources added *on top of* the base gates. They are folded in detail into §2–§7,
> but I list them here so none is ever skipped. Each has a § pointer to its full worked treatment.

1. **Value created per ₹1 retained** = ΔMcap(10y) ÷ cumulative retained earnings(10y). **≥1 required; <1 = capital incinerator, no P/E is low enough** (Rain 0.31, Datamatics 0.48, Just Dial **−7.64**). Discard the metric on a tiny-RE denominator (India Glycols) or a distressed near-zero starting Mcap (Marksans 23.35 artifact). → §2[V6], §5[V6], §5[V8]
2. **FCFE = FCF − interest > 0.** If negative, dividends/buybacks/growth are debt-funded no matter how strong CFO looks (Rain −₹3,710cr). → §2[V7]
3. **SSGR vs achieved growth**, plus the structural version: **low NFAT (~≤1.5) × low NPM (~≤4%) = a debt trap that can never self-fund** — do the incremental-growth math before believing any growth story. → §4, §2[V7]
4. **Decompose every cCFO-vs-cPAT gap** before crediting or damning it. Benign: other income (§2[V4]); interest+depreciation add-back in a levered firm (§2[V7]); a *deliberate* WC-intensive moat (AIA §2[V8]). Malignant: rising receivable-days/inventory with no operating reason (§3). The headline ratio lies until decomposed.
5. **Margin-source triage on every OPM change:** real pricing power vs cost-efficiency vs external luck. **Pricing power is proven ONLY by RM-cost-%-of-sales FALLING while OPM rises** (Atul 59→48%); lagged pass-through, cartel/duty props, competitor outages (IOL/BASF), and currency (IT services) are NOT pricing power. → §2[V3], §2[V8], §6
6. **Cyclical? Value ONLY on mid-cycle/normalized earnings** — trailing P/E looks cheapest at the earnings peak (HEG 2.78, Rain 5.79). Never extrapolate an up-cycle. → §2[V5], §5[V7]
7. **Both P/E tails are traps.** Low P/E: would it pass every gate at a *normal* P/E? If not, the cheapness is the market's correct verdict (§5[V5]–[V7]). High P/E (≳40): model the de-rating scenario explicitly — Just Dial P/E 65→7 *while PAT doubled* (§5[V8]). Quality without margin of safety is still an AVOID (Honeywell 48, Supreme 42.5, Atul 32, AIA 31).
8. **PBT ÷ avg NFA vs the FD rate** — a business earning less on its assets than a fixed deposit has no reason to exist; also benchmark PBT/NFA against peers. → §2[V4]
9. **Governance-density counter:** sweep §7b + the 278-item forensic catalog; **≥3 independent governance tricks = REJECT regardless of price** (Marksans). One flag may have an excuse; a *density* of flags is itself the signal. → §7[V8]
10. **Consolidated-first, with the two scope checks:** (a) the NBFC/finance-subsidiary exception — analyze the manufacturer standalone + NBFC separately (Ashok Leyland §2[V7]); (b) confirm the consolidation *perimeter* — entities left out at management discretion (Minda ~20% of group turnover) or subsidiaries appearing/disappearing YoY (Marksans GmbH) are red flags. → §0, §2[V7], §7[V8]
11. **[V10] Mitigant-vs-cap test on every "resilient margin" story:** engineered margin-defense mechanics (short-window price-list resets, channel financing, converter/fixed fees, customer-funded plants, rate discipline, recycling, fuel-flex) are *cushions to score one notch* — NOT pricing power and NOT a moat (§6 still requires falling-RM%/rate-discipline/switching-cost proof); and identify any **structural CAP** that overrules operator quality — state-regulated sin sectors (Godfrey: value-per-₹-RE **3.96** despite FCF ₹2,051cr — the cap, quantified), regulator-rented moats (Sharda stroke-of-a-pen), donor/tender price erosion (Laurus ARV), duty-propped margins on can't-idle assets (Asahi). **In a capped sector the low P/E is the cap priced correctly, and the value-per-₹-RE ceiling ≈4 is the expected outcome, not a bargain.** → §2[V10], §6[V10], §27
12. **[V10] Owner-extraction scan (mandatory when the owner is a PE fund, MNC parent, or feuding family):** trend R&D-%-of-sales and headcount over 10 yrs (Accelya: 10%→0.2–1% and 1,591→1,248 under three PE owners), dividend payout >100% (124–185%), MD/CEO churn, takeover-year salary jumps (+80%), royalty/brand fees, parent-priced asset deals (AGC sold its sick co INTO Asahi then recovered ₹225cr loans), dividends paid while FCF is negative (= debt/equity-funded: Laurus, Safari). **The owner's cash needs are not the listco's growth plan; when the owner milks, the asset decays by choice and the exit is the owner's, not yours.** → §7[V10], §27
13. **[V11] SME-IPO structural screen (run BEFORE any financial analysis of a small/recent listing):** (a) issue sized just under oversight thresholds (<₹100cr = no monitoring agency; rights <₹50cr = no SEBI review — Varanium did BOTH); (b) project 100% IPO-funded with zero debt and zero promoter money = no lender ever appraised it, no skin in the game; (c) auditor changed every year pre-IPO / disclaimed opinions; (d) promoter's name googled — arrests, prior scams, arbitration awards omitted from "Litigation: Nil" (Varanium's promoter: 2009 cheating arrest, Malta data-centre scandal, the Rolta fake-₹5,500cr-investment episode, an HC arrest order); (e) pledge exploding post-listing (0→64% in a year); (f) PAT-to-CFO chasm alongside mega-acquisition announcements (₹178cr PAT / ₹8cr CFO / ₹2,000cr+ acquisition plan); (g) the lock-in expiry date on the calendar = the dump window. **Any two of these = walk away; SEBI's order arrives only after the money is gone.** → §3, §7[V11], §28
14. **[V11] ACCOUNTING-REGIME REBUILD before trusting any margin/cash trend:** (a) **IndAS-115 netting** — pass-through cost (power, trade discounts) removed from BOTH revenue and expense fakes an OPM jump with profit unchanged (Linde 15%→24% in FY2019 was the standard, not the business; PGHH's A&P looks lower post-IndAS because ~6% trade discounts moved into net revenue) — gross-up before comparing across the transition; (b) **exceptional-gains-vs-cPAT test**: cumulative exceptional items ÷ cumulative PAT-before-exceptionals — Linde ₹1,092cr vs ₹1,707cr (64%!) means the "business" was substantially an asset-liquidation annuity; (c) **write-back-earnings test**: profits built from creditors' losses (OTS interest waivers, sundry balances written back — Websol's whole profit history) are terminal-stage noise, not earnings; (d) **CMLTD-inside-CFO**: trace "other current/financial liabilities" inflows in CFO to current-maturity-of-long-term-debt (Ramco ₹41.3cr FY15, Rushil ₹21.5cr FY21 — CFO inflated, CFF flattered — extends §2[V9] rebuild). → §2[V11], §3, §28
15. **[V12] STORY-STOCK / ANNOUNCEMENT-MACHINE screen (run at STEP 0 on any SME or theme stock whose chart has re-rated >5× in ≤2 years):** count the fingerprints — (a) **shell lineage**: the listco was acquired as a listed shell and renamed / object clause switched to the season's theme (Nibe: fan-trading shell → "defence" via EGM object switch; TechNvision: Ankur Agencies → Solix → TechNvision); (b) **announcement cadence ≫ delivery**: MoUs, foreign tie-ups, "orders", award/badge PRs (Gartner quadrants, "Top AI Executives"), even conference-registration openings filed as EXCHANGE announcements (TechNvision), with the Urja-Global "Zacobite" fake-mineral SEBI order as the proven archetype — cross-check every announcement against an independent source (the counterparty, government records) before crediting ANY of them; (c) **warrants/preferentials timed to the price run** (Nibe allotted ~₹500 → exercised ~₹2,000; Advait ₹82.3cr preferential at ₹1,388 near the top; the 25/75 warrant free-option, Captain re-issuing after lapses); (d) **promoters/insiders SELLING into the rally the announcements created** (RIR: ₹29cr across the family at ₹835–2,587 post-Odisha-news; the independent director liquidating 100%; Varanium's lock-in-expiry dump §28); (e) **the asset base doesn't match the story** (Nibe: "defence manufacturer" with ₹21 lakh gross block and fixed-assets = 0.00 on screener for years; land bought only AFTER the run began; majority TRADED-goods revenue); (f) **incomplete/contradictory exchange filings** (Tembo filed ARs missing sections; warrants announcements with wrong dates/counts refiled next day — Advait). **Any TWO of (a)–(f) = REJECT without further analysis; the P/E (Nibe 273→89, RIR 228, TechNvision 326, Wonder 163) is measuring the story, not a business.** → §29, §7[V12], §3
16. **[V12] REVENUE-MIX REBUILD (trading-in-disguise test) — before accepting ANY "manufacturer/defence/tech/green-energy" label, rebuild revenue by segment from the AR notes:** (a) **majority trading revenue at ~1–2% segment margin = value it as a trader**, whatever the name says (Tembo "Powering Ahead" defence-pumps story: **76% of FY24 revenue was TRADING of fabrics/textiles at 2% margin**; Nibe FY24: ₹161cr traded vs ₹43cr manufactured); (b) **NFAT >10–20 is itself a trading tell** — pure manufacturers usually run NFAT ≤5–6; a high NFAT means thin value-add, makes SSGR non-meaningful, and the "manufacturing" story suspect (Captain 20.4, del-credere IOCL agency segment running at a LOSS); (c) **check what the revenue actually was in the early years of the story** (KPI's first "solar" revenues: SALE OF PLOT ₹23.5cr of ₹25.8cr FY17 revenue — a land developer wearing a solar costume); (d) corollary: **the SSGR pairing rule** — SSGR is only valid alongside cPAT≈cCFO AND normal NFAT; growth within SSGR still needs external money when working capital eats the profits (Dynamic: SSGR 25% > growth 13%, yet debt +₹70cr and a ₹96.6cr preferential because receivables+inventory consumed ₹294cr). → §29, §2[V12], §4
