  - **Good divisions hived OUT without independent bidding:** *Century Textiles* demerged its cement to sister **UltraTech** and leased its rayon to sister **Grasim** (both Aditya-Birla-group) with only a CA-firm valuation minorities openly disputed ("get competing bids"; 18.61% voted against). No auction = assume the price favours the group, not the minority.
  - **Cash routed OUT via inter-corporate deposits while the listco itself carries bank debt:** *Ashok Leyland* → Hinduja Energy ₹615cr/₹463cr; *Honeywell* → Honeywell Turbo ₹509cr; *Heidelberg* asked to lend ₹50cr to sister Zuari; (cf. National Peroxide/Paushak §7[V6]). A manufacturer paying interest on bank loans while lending to group entities is subsidising the group.
  - **Buying out promoters/PE at bad times:** *Ashok Leyland* moved to buy 19% of HLFL when the PE investor held only 7% (i.e. giving *promoters* an exit) mid-downturn — the market crashed the stock 26%, forcing a cut to 6.99%.
  - **Consolidation-avoidance to keep stakes/losses off-book:** *Ashok Leyland* treats a **62%-held** company as a "JV" (not subsidiary) by contract, and a 26% stake as "no significant influence"; *Rain* folds **unaudited** subsidiary financials (₹1,038cr revenue) into consolidated accounts. When you invest in a group company, assume this shuffling *will* happen and price it in.
- **A "NET CASH" balance sheet that still needed a promoter BAILOUT to meet an obligation = the strength is fake.** *Ashok Leyland* reported net-cash of ₹731–2,915cr in its annual reports, yet admitted in a call that the Hinduja group had to "bail us out" (buy HLFL tranches from Everstone) because it was cash-crunched — the classic Cox & Kings / Yes Bank / DHFL / IL&FS tell where reported cash isn't really available. Cross-check claimed liquidity against actual behaviour under stress.
- **SERIAL "throwing good money after bad" is a management-quality veto (not a one-off mistake).** The tell is **impairment AND fresh investment in the SAME loss-maker in the same year**, repeated for years. *Ashok Leyland* is the archetype: **Optare** (bought 26%→99%, losses every year FY14–19 ≈ ₹1,000cr, impaired ₹150cr then lent ₹169cr more the same year, still funding ₹80–90cr/yr), **Albonair**, **John Deere JV** (liquidated at a ₹233cr loss yet kept funding), **Nissan LCV JV** (partner Nissan cut its losses and sold its half for ₹1 while ALL kept pouring in), **Avia**. Related tells: **"held for sale" reclassification** to park a loss-maker out of consolidation then flip back to "held for use" when no buyer appears; **converting a subsidiary loan to equity** = admitting the loan won't be repaid (a disguised write-off). (*Rain*/Tarlog and *ADF*/Elena's-US show the same.) A cash-rich business that won't cut losses will bleed for a decade.
- **DEBT-FUNDED DIVIDENDS (money is fungible).** If a company's FCF is below its interest bill yet it still pays dividends while debt rises, the dividend is effectively borrowed. *Rain* (FCF ₹1,065cr < interest ₹4,775cr → dividends/buybacks debt-funded; FCFE −₹3,710cr) and *Century* (paid ₹51cr/yr dividends through FY11–15, including the FY13 *loss* year, while debt rose ₹2,369cr→₹6,139cr) both did this. A dividend is only real if it comes from FCF-after-interest.
- **A promoter WARRANT is a free option skewed to the promoter, not a capital infusion — and the abuse has three forms.** (i) **25%-upfront / 75%-on-exercise** lets promoters convert only if the stock rises (heads-I-win) and walk from the 75% if it falls (tails-you-lose) — SEBI raised the upfront from 10%→25% in Feb-2009 for exactly this. (ii) **Lapse-then-reissue-cheaper:** *ADF* let ₹70 warrants lapse in the 2008 crash, then *immediately* reissued at ₹32 to recoup the promoters' loss at minorities' expense. (iii) **The company self-financing the "infusion":** *ADF* paid out inter-corporate deposits ≈ the warrant-exercise amount *each time* promoters exercised (money out → comes back as "promoter money"). Promoters also monetise gains by selling *existing* shares (only the warrant shares are locked) — *Century* promoters booked ₹240cr+ paper gains and sold old stock. If a promoter genuinely wants to fund the company, they subscribe shares at market for 100% upfront.
- **MNC-parent extraction runs through overhead/royalty/ICD channels, and a parent-linked credit rating overstates standalone strength.** *Honeywell* charged **₹873cr of unexplained "corporate overhead allocations"** (a transfer-pricing black box) plus travel at **7–8% of sales vs 1–4% for IT/electronics peers**, and lent ICDs to group entities. *Heidelberg*'s rating was *upgraded because its parent's rating improved* (centralised treasury, cross-default, debt down-streamed from the parent), its MD is paid directly by the parent, and it was asked to lend to sister Zuari. For any MNC subsidiary, quantify the overhead/royalty %, benchmark discretionary costs vs peers, and read what the rating actually rests on.
- **INTEGRITY VETOES — reject on any of these regardless of the numbers:** **promoter/KMP insider trading** in the company's own shares via relatives' demat accounts, SEBI-penalised (*ADF*'s ED/CFO Bhavesh Thakkar, ₹1.02cr impounded — the money-trail ran through his wife → mother-in-law → wife's-cousin accounts); **a SEBI-barred market manipulator holding a large block** (*ADF* — Sanjay Dangi's entities ~22%, via Authum/Mentor Capital; he was barred for promoter-broker-operator price-rigging) — always identify who the big non-promoter holders *are*; **hidden-promoter concert parties** inflating true control (*Filatex* — same-surname "friends & relatives" (Vimal/Ankit Bhageria entities) repeatedly get preferential warrants but aren't classified as promoters → true control ≈ **75.6% vs disclosed 58%**; trace preferential-allotment recipients through director databases).
- **ACCOUNTING PROPS to inflate profit — strip each one and believe the auditor's qualification over the headline:** **capitalising forex losses** into fixed assets instead of the P&L (*Filatex* — auditor-qualified; NSE/SEBI ordered a restatement, only quashed on appeal by SAT; cf. HEG §2[V5]); **routing losses/write-offs to a reserve to bypass the P&L** (*Ashok Leyland* charged ₹160cr of asset/investment diminution to the securities-premium account via court approval; cf. Escorts BRR §7[V6]); **recurring commodity write-downs disguised as "Exceptional Items"** every single year (*Rain* — inventory write-downs ₹23–90cr yearly + doubtful-debt provisions labelled exceptional); **brand revaluation booked as profit** (*ADF* revalued its "Ashoka" brand and took ₹7.24cr to the P&L as an exceptional gain; cf. Indo Count §5[V2]); **depreciation-policy changes** that lift profit (*Ashok Leyland* +₹21cr).
- **Management pay UNLINKED to performance or role = capital-allocation indifference.** *Century*'s whole-time-director pay rose ~15%/yr (₹1.61cr→₹3.74cr) while PAT went from ₹339cr profit to a ₹95cr *loss* — including a +23% hike in a loss year. *Ashok Leyland* handed its CEO **56% of all ESOPs at a ₹1 exercise price** (all other staff competed for the rest at ₹80–109). *Paushak*'s **non-executive** promoter son out-earns both the chairman and the full-time CEO (+62% in a year PAT fell). When pay is divorced from results/contribution, no one internalises bad capital allocation until the debt trap springs.
- **RPT TIMING GAMES make the current annual report un-trustworthy.** *Rain* discloses related-party transactions a *year late* (a ₹443cr advance/₹396cr refund with promoter-entity REPL, and ₹305–365cr/yr with promoter trucking-co ALPL, appear only in the *next* year's report — ALPL wasn't even listed as a related party the year it transacted), and buys "raw material" from a promoter *cinema* company. If RPTs surface a year after the fact, you can never trust that this year's report is complete — raise the bar to a veto.
- **[V8] REVERSE-RPT — a cash-rich company borrowing FROM its promoters at an above-market rate is an extraction channel (interest to the promoter), and a loan TO a promoter/minority is the classic one.** Watch both directions. *Atul* is cash-rich and debt-light yet took unsecured loans *from* the promoter group at **~10%** — paying promoters interest it did not need to; it also **sold its reviving Amal subsidiary back to the promoters** (giving away a turnaround). *AIA* extended a loan to a minority shareholder and diluted subsidiary stakes unexplained while sitting on cash. **Any money moving between listco and promoter — in *either* direction, and on any pretext (loan, interest, asset sale, JV) — is a related-party flag; a *cash-rich* company that still transacts loans with promoters is choosing to enrich them (extends §7[V6] listco-as-ATM to the reverse-loan case).**
- **[V8] NOT booking MTM / forex / derivative losses (auditor-flagged) is profit *overstatement* — believe the auditor's qualification, add the loss back.** Vol 8 shows the same manipulation three times: *AIA* did **not** mark its outstanding derivative/forex positions to market (auditor-flagged) → reported profit overstated; *Amber* carried unbooked MTM losses; *Marksans* did **not** book FCCB foreign-exchange losses (auditor-qualified). This is the mirror of *Filatex* capitalising forex losses (§7[V7]) — same intent (keep the loss out of the P&L), different mechanic. **Whenever the auditor qualifies the accounts over un-recognised MTM/forex/derivative losses, restate: pull the loss into earnings before valuing, and downgrade management integrity.**
- **[V8] The REVERSE conglomerate-shuffle — junk / accumulated LOSSES pushed INTO the listco — plus warrant-lapse-abuse and perpetual dilution, is the small-cap extraction playbook.** Where Vol 7's shuffle hived *good* assets *out* (Century→UltraTech), Vol 8's small-caps push *bad* things *in*: *Just Dial* shifted a loss-making "JD Global" data division into the listco (**~95% of its assets were deferred-tax on accumulated losses** — i.e. it imported losses to shelter listco tax while diluting minorities); *IOL Chemicals* **merged a bankrupt promoter entity (G Drugs, net worth −₹3.48cr) into itself, paying the promoters fresh shares**, and separately **let promoter warrants lapse — forfeiting the upfront money — while the company was defaulting on its PNB loan** (warrant games §7 tag), on top of near-*annual* equity dilution. *Minda* overpaid to buy promoter assets (AutoGas — **impaired ₹109cr right after purchase**), inserted the promoter into JVs, and ran ₹130cr/yr of buy+sell through promoter partnership firms. **Any of: importing promoter losses/junk, warrant forfeiture while defaulting, buying a promoter asset that is promptly impaired, or perpetual dilution → veto.**
- **[V8] The manipulation-DENSE distress playbook (pharma/turnaround) — when you count *this many* tricks in one name, it is a hard veto no matter how cheap or "recovered."** *Marksans Pharma* stacks: a BIFR bankruptcy "recovery" that was a **creditor write-back (FCCB haircut ~₹227cr), not operational** (§22 low-P/E-trap on a distressed base); **reduce-then-reinstate subsidiary valuations to game BIFR entry/exit**; an **80%-impaired intangible black box** (₹137cr→₹27.7cr, unexplained); a **hidden subsidiary omitted from the annual reports** (Marksans GmbH absent FY15–18); a **concealed-front FCCB buyback** via an undisclosed entity (Yalegrove); and years of **CSR non-compliance**. Independently, *Sonata* carries a **disputed tax liability of ₹513cr (~2× annual PAT)** and an unexplained "insourcing professional fees" black box, and *Amber*'s PE investor **transferred shares to the promoters at ₹1 after buying at ₹237** plus impersonation-fraud/weak-controls and chronic delayed statutory dues. **Rule: red flags COMPOUND — one might be explained away, but a *density* of independent governance tricks (hidden entities, valuation games, unexplained impairments, front entities, statutory-dues defaults) is itself the signal. Count them; three or more independent tricks = reject.**
- **[V9] The EQUITY-RAISE→ICD FUNNEL: a company that doesn't need money raises it preferentially, then immediately lends it OUT to unnamed parties — the listco as a money-moving channel.** *Lincoln Pharma* had SSGR 25–35% vs 9% growth (no funding need) yet raised **₹70.5cr** via serial preferential shares + promoter warrants (FY05–FY17) — and in FY2006 raised ₹5.5cr → gave ₹5.15cr as ICDs; FY2011 raised ₹20.5cr → ₹16.7cr out as ICDs; "loans to others" stood at **₹44cr** by FY2021, counterparties unnamed. **Any pattern of raise-then-lend-out = treat the listco as a conduit; veto.** (Related tell: audit fees jumped 3.7× in the year of the big pass-through.)
- **[V9] Merging promoter companies INTO the listco — always COMPUTE the implied P/E/P-B paid; and watch for convert-and-dump.** *Valiant Organics* absorbed two Aarti-group promoter entities: ATCL at **P/E 35–45 / P-B 10.4** (₹184.6cr of shares for a ₹4.2–5.5cr-PAT business) and Amarjyot at **P/E ~58 / P-B >20** (~₹1,500cr of shares+OCPS for ₹25.9cr PAT / ₹73.7cr net worth); promoters then **converted OCPS and sold 5.37% within days (₹219cr)**, having also taken the IPO as a **pure offer-for-sale** and paid **debt-funded dividends** (dividends doubled while FCF was negative). *Lincoln* bought promoter-owned LPRL in three tranches at **P/E 48 → 103 → 151** *while LPRL's sales fell from ₹111cr to ₹44cr*. **Rule: for every promoter-entity merger/purchase, derive the multiple from the merged entity's own accounts — a rich multiple paid in listco shares is dilution-based extraction (the valuation-quantified version of §7[V8]'s junk-pushed-in).**
- **[V9] SAME-BRAND promoter competitors and promoter-owned brand/domain assets: check WHO owns what the listco's business runs on.** *SOM Distilleries*: the promoters' **private** SDPL sells the *same IMFL brands* (Milestone 100, White Fox, Gypsy, Blue Chip) as the listco — ownership of the brands and the split of business is opaque, and SDPL sat as a 9.77% "promoter" shareholder while being **de-classified from related parties for two years (FY16–FY17), hiding all transactions with it**. *Monte Carlo*: the montecarlo.in **domain is owned by promoter co Oswal Woollen Mills**, retail EBOs + e-commerce were run through promoter co Vanaik Spinning (shifting the retail profit pool), and 90–95% of wool comes from the promoter mill. **Rule: list the brands/domains/plants the business depends on and verify the LISTCO owns them; a promoter twin selling the same brands = veto** (extends §22 Shri-Jagdamba parallel-firm).
- **[V9] The INTEREST-FREE promoter current-account — including borrowing at interest to fund it — and guarantees the auditor isn't shown.** *SOM* ran a bank-style current account with promoter co SDPL (peak **₹46cr** due, explicitly interest-free), and in FY2015 **borrowed ₹55cr while advances to SDPL rose ₹15cr** — then blamed "interest cost" for the profit decline; it also gave corporate guarantees for group loans that were **omitted from contingent liabilities** one year and **refused to the auditor** in another (FY2014: "in the absence of necessary information, we are unable to comment"), and ₹0.43cr of share-application money to promoter co Som Power sat 8 years, then **silently vanished with no shares received**. **Any of these = the listco is the group's float; veto** (extends §7[V6] ATM).
- **[V9] BENCHMARK marketing/promotion spend and "advances" against peers — an outsized, ineffective spend routed to counterparties is a siphon pattern.** *SOM* spent **11–15% of revenue on "sales promotion"** every year (₹338cr over the decade) against **5–6% at United Breweries/Radico (5–10× larger) and 0.2–0.3% at GM Breweries/Globus (same size)** — and paid it substantially as **advances TO its retailers** (₹20–40cr; customers should advance the company, not the reverse), kept spending through liquidity stress and 4%-growth years. *Asian Energy* spiked business promotion ₹0.6→7.4cr to buy one year's revenue jump. **Rule: promo % of sales >2–3× peer norm + advances flowing the wrong way = investigate as extraction, not marketing.**
- **[V9] CRIMINAL/ARREST history and political entanglement are veto-tier, not "monitor":** *SOM's* promoters were **arrested in 2020 (₹33.5cr GST evasion on sanitizers in their private co)** and in **2001 (attack on Income-Tax officials to snatch seized documents)**, plus a fake ₹3cr demand draft in a liquor auction; *PNC Infratech's* promoter is a **BJP mayor** (political-cycle risk cuts both ways: bonus scrutiny by opposition; CBI raided in 2012 airport-tender case); *Deepak Nitrite* carries a pending **CBI corruption case** (1995 discount) and an FY2019 Income-Tax raid. **Promoter rap sheet = reject; political alignment = model the regime-change scenario.**
- **[V9] Small self-granted ACCOUNTING favours that inflate profit: booking an OTS benefit before approval, creditor's loss as income, relabeling dues, and "zero bad debt ever."** *SOM* recognized the **lower OTS interest rate from FY2005 while MPSIDC only accepted the settlement in FY2011** (understated interest for 6 years) and booked the **₹8.8cr bank haircut as P&L income** (cf. Marksans write-back §25); *Beekay* re-labeled the same unpaid **undisputed** tax dues as "**disputed**" from FY2018 (erasing the compliance flag) and its tax-reconciliation table doesn't reconcile; *NGL* showed **zero bad debt for 12 straight years** (the only such company of hundreds analyzed — implausible) and two annual reports state **contradictory receivables-ageing for the same year** (92.5% vs 7.5% >6mo for FY2011); *Monte Carlo* claimed "zero bad debt" then wrote off ₹2.71cr days after management said "100% confident" of recovery. **Each is small; each is intent. They count toward the density veto.**
- **[V9] Tax-integrity tells: hawala/fake purchase invoices (ITAT-upheld) and profit-shifting into a tax-holiday subsidiary.** *NGL Fine Chem* was held by the Income-Tax Appellate Tribunal to have **inflated expenses with bogus invoices from hawala dealers**; *Jamna Auto* faced repeated IT orders for **selling to its 80-IC tax-exempt Pantnagar subsidiary at suppressed margins** to shift profit into the tax holiday (settled via amnesty schemes = paid up). **A company that fakes costs or games intra-group transfer pricing against the taxman will game minority shareholders too.**
- **[V9] Family-payroll extraction and both-direction promoter loans:** *Lincoln* pays **HUFs, wives and daughters of directors ~₹1.5cr/yr in "commissions" and salaries** (some spouses not even disclosed as related parties in earlier ARs) and routes sales through promoters' Allantis Exim while financing via promoters' Downtown Finance; *Monte Carlo* pays the CMD's daughters ₹1cr each (₹15cr cumulative) while **excluding them from the senior-management list** (pay without an evidenced role); *Jamna's* chairman drew pay in a year he attended **zero board meetings**, then his remuneration moved into a subsidiary LLP (out of standalone sight); *Beekay* carries **₹25–50cr of unsecured loans FROM directors/corporates at undisclosed interest rates**. **Map every rupee flowing to the family beyond disclosed executive pay; undisclosed-rate loans in either direction are extraction channels.**

**Verdict:** management is a **hard veto.** If integrity is in any doubt (fraud history, self-dealing RPT, **reverse-RPT: loans/interest paid TO promoters while cash-rich, assets sold back to promoters**, warrant abuse (incl. **letting warrants lapse while defaulting to lenders**), debt-funded dividends to self, fake-independent boards, parallel competing businesses, data falsification, rating-shopping, **not-booking MTM/forex/derivative losses (auditor-flagged profit overstatement)**, **listco-as-group-ATM: ICDs/loans to promoter entities, promoter-owned suppliers, sell-through-promoter firms, asymmetric promoter-JV valuations**, **the conglomerate shuffle in EITHER direction: junk / accumulated LOSSES pushed IN (Just Dial JD-Global, IOL's bankrupt G-Drugs merger) / good assets hived OUT to sister cos without bidding / overpaid promoter-asset buys promptly impaired (Minda AutoGas) / net-cash-that-needed-a-bailout / serial "good money after bad" / insider trading / hidden concert-party control**, **a DENSITY of independent tricks: hidden/omitted subsidiaries, concealed front entities, reduce-then-reinstate valuations, unexplained intangible black-box impairments, perpetual dilution, chronic delayed statutory dues — three or more = reject**) → **REJECT**, even with flawless financials/valuation. "Investment in a great business is futile if management isn't shareholder-friendly." Never trust awards/ratings as proof of integrity.
**[V9] additions to the veto list:** **equity-raise→lend-out-as-ICD funnels; promoter companies merged into the listco at rich computed multiples (± convert-OCPS-and-dump); a promoter twin selling the SAME brands / owning the listco's domain or retail channel; interest-free promoter current-accounts (esp. funded by fresh listco debt); guarantees hidden from contingent liabilities or refused to the auditor; capex that created no capacity; promo-spend far above peer norms paid as advances TO customers; promoter arrests/criminal record; hawala invoices or transfer-pricing games against the taxman; family payroll (HUFs/spouses/children) without evidenced roles; undisclosed-rate loans to or from directors; booking settlement benefits before approval; relabeling undisputed dues as disputed.**
**[V10] additions to the veto list:** **paying promoter entities' statutory liabilities by re-pricing old purchases (Godfrey revised historical cigarette prices so its Assam promoter-suppliers could meet excise demands — a ₹27.7cr receivable became ₹62cr paid OUT, a ~₹90cr shareholder swing); buying the promoters' disputed/undevelopable land into the listco (Rajputana AOP: ₹23.2cr interest-free loan in FY08, then a staged buy-out of the promoters from land idle 12 years); family remuneration musical chairs — one member waives, another doubles (KK Modi waives → Samir +116% to ₹10.6cr, later ₹26.8cr), pay without work (Lalit Modi ₹1–2cr/yr as non-executive attending ZERO board meetings; son Ruchir appointed at 22 at ₹1.5cr), and pay routed via a sister co (Bina + Charu Modi ₹17cr EACH from unlisted Indofil while drawing ~nil from the listco) — SUM THE FAMILY'S TOTAL TAKE ACROSS ALL GROUP COMPANIES (Godfrey: 2%→6–7% of PAT; cf. Sharda Motor's Relan pattern §26); a succession war conducted through regulators (letters to SEBI/MCA → central-govt inspection of the books → complainant director removed → reported ₹11,000cr family cash settlement) = the listco is the family battlefield; associate profits NEVER consolidated behind an unquantified "prior losses exceed investment" (Godfrey-IPM: ₹440cr cumulative Marlboro-JV profit, share never booked, loss quantum never disclosed — a permanent black box); NOT booking interest on defaulted debt "under negotiation" (Lloyds, ₹100cr+ scale → PBT fiction), booking the lender's haircut as P&L income, and an MGT-9 indebtedness table showing ZERO while the balance sheet carries debt; settling an arbitration award by issuing OFCDs (11.89%) to a promoter-linked counterparty (Lloyds → Shree Global Tradefin — which also sells goods to the listco); the MNC-parent double-dip: parent merges its SICK company into the listco then recovers its own loans from the merged entity (AGC/Asahi ₹225cr) on top of royalties/fees; the PE-owner milking fingerprint (kill-test #12: R&D→~0, shrinking headcount, >100% payouts, MD churn, takeover-year pay hikes — Accelya); warrants to the promoter at a discount to market, sized to restore control to EXACTLY 51.02% after a dilution (Safari — the 25/75 asymmetry §7[CS] with a control motive); dividends declared while FCF is negative = debt/equity-funded dividends (Safari ₹6cr on FCF −₹102cr; Laurus ₹292cr on FCF −₹535cr); registrations/brands the business runs on held in third parties' names with no binding agreement (Sharda).**
**[V11] additions to the veto list:** **the promoter SHARE ROUND-TRIP — listco sells shares of a promoter co to the promoters cheap, then buys the same company back dear via merger (Relaxo: 6,040 RRPL shares sold FY16 for ₹4.32cr → merged back FY19 at ~₹14cr in stock; the merger itself paid ₹270cr in shares for entities with ₹153cr of gross assets, to save ₹6.2cr of rent); remuneration at the statutory ceiling TO THE LAST DECIMAL with family-slab equal pay (Relaxo FY16: limit ₹19.3137cr, paid ₹19.3137cr; brothers exactly ₹8.79875cr each; four next-gen members exactly ₹1.3176cr each — pay by bloodline, not merit); SUING THE REGULATOR to stop an investigation (Linde's Bombay-HC writ against the SEBI probe that followed a 93.95% shareholder vote-down of its promoter-RPT resolution — both the vote and the writ are the market's verdict); disclosure DIFFS between the exchange-filed audit report and the annual-report copy of the SAME report (Linde's RPT-limit observation and Websol's noncompliance list both vanished from the AR copies — diff them); the CROSS-HOLDING stake-inflation loop with worked math (Ramco: listco's 21.36% of Ramco Cements counts as 'promoter' holding while promoters own only 53.88% of the listco → ₹1 of promoter money controls ₹2 of group stake; ICD round-trips fund the purchases; group companies bought promoters' shares giving them exit liquidity at stake-neutral optics); the SOLE-SELLING-AGENT rent — promoters' own trust takes 1% of ALL sales as commission (Ramco: ₹61cr/10yrs to Raja Charity Trust, whose trustees are the chairman and MD… who also draw salaries, and the listco even LENDS to the trust); rights issues at deep discounts converting PROMOTER loans to equity with additional-subscription rights (Rushil ×2: stake 53.24%→59.86%, while management simultaneously called its 0.85% debt too cheap to prepay); an RHP falsehood fined by SEBI (Rushil: IPO proceeds repaid an undisclosed pre-IPO bridge loan the RHP denied having); rating-agency shopping in quick succession (Rushil: CARE outlook cut → India Ratings A− within 2 months → 3-notch downgrade in 8 months → switched to Infomerics for BBB+); independent-director theatre (an ID whose family held 9.55% pre-IPO; a 26-year-old CA-Inter ID whose disclosed skill was reading basic financial statements); GROUP-BANK behavior at MNCs too — P&G Hygiene lent ₹2,200cr to group entities in one year (₹1,911cr to the unlisted arm) plus 1996–2010 ICDs to counterparties never named in the RPT schedule, Abbott lent ₹200cr to a group co and breached RPT limits ratified after the fact; the ROYALTY RAMP (PGHH 1.8%→5.4% of sales while listco R&D = nil); a COMFORT LETTER for the promoter's wife's personal property loan (Websol → LICHFL, ₹5.9cr); pay rising through loss/default years (Websol +119% during a 5-loss-year default stretch, ₹1.32cr in a ZERO-revenue year, granddaughter at 26 appointed ED+CFO at ₹1cr; Rushil bonuses in the FY15 loss year, +96.7% hikes, son's contract with MINIMUM 20% annual increments; Ramco's 28-year-old MD at ₹8.24cr = 7% of ex-associates PAT with the AR admitting "no relationship between remuneration and performance").**
**[V12] additions to the veto list:** **the LISTED-SHELL takeover + object-clause switch + serial rename to the season's theme (Nibe: a fan-trading shell whose promoters assembled control for ₹3.48cr, switched objects to "defence" by EGM, and rode announcements to a ₹2,650cr Mcap; TechNvision: Ankur Agencies → Solix Technologies → TechNvision Ventures); the ANNOUNCEMENT MACHINE as a management style — filing conference-registration openings, speaker lists, badge awards ("Gartner Visionary", "Top AI Executives of 2024") and MoUs as exchange announcements while cumulative profits are ~zero (TechNvision: 23-year net LOSS of ₹6.4cr; Urja/Zacobite the SEBI-proven archetype — the mineral did not exist); PROMOTERS SELLING INTO THE ANNOUNCEMENT RALLY they created (RIR/Mehta family ₹29cr at ₹835–2,587 after the Odisha-fab news — with the INDEPENDENT DIRECTOR liquidating his entire 7,000 shares; a director resigning by handwritten letter citing 'busy schedule' as the run peaked — Tembo/Vandrevala); the WOS CARVE-OUT — a subsidiary created inside the listco is taken over PERSONALLY by promoters just before the theme monetizes (Tembo), and its cousin the STEP-DOWN STAKE LEAK — the listco's stake in the operating step-downs quietly diluted (TechNvision: Solix/Emagia 68.37%→58.21%/63.15%) with the counterparty owners UNDISCLOSED; the MEHTA RPT PLAYBOOK — sell the listco a defective plant from a promoter entity, let the promoter entity go bankrupt and take it back cheap, then sell the listco ₹42cr of UNDEVELOPED IP priced by a replacement-cost valuation (110% overhead markup + 20% developer profit + 19.5% RARR on man-months — a fiction dressed as a fairness opinion — RIR/Sicamore); the PROMOTER GOODWILL BUYOUT — the listco pays the promoters for 'goodwill' of a business the same promoters already run inside it (Wonder: ₹23.42cr in 5% NCRPS for ₹8.24cr of net assets → ₹15.18cr of goodwill paid to themselves); the PROMOTER-LAND FLYWHEEL + ROYALTY RENT — listco's projects sit on land bought from/through the promoter family (KPI: ₹50.26cr 'advance for land' to the CMD in one year, plus round-trip advances given and received back) and the listco pays the promoter a personal ROYALTY for the brand name (KPI: ₹20.42cr in FY24 alone); MUTUAL cross-holdings between two listed group cos for vote inflation (Captain Polyplast ↔ Captain Pipes each holding 13–17% of the other — promoters control both blocks with half the money; the associate even subscribes the listco's warrants); the listco as the HOLDING company's bridge financier — borrowing to lend upward ('expected to render support… group company obligations': TechNvision took a ₹14cr loan to hand Tiebeam ₹23cr as a property advance + loan, returned within months = free bridge finance; liabilities to 'Directors & Relatives' on the balance sheet but MISSING from the RPT schedule); REVOLVING promoter loans at above-market rates as a permanent income stream (Dynamic: 16.85% paid to the promoter, in-and-out every year; Captain: 12%; RIR: 12.5%); the 40%-OF-NET-PROFIT pay authorization (Wonder: shareholder-approved cap raised from the statutory 11% to 40% — promoters took 40% of PAT in a weak year at Captain too); RETAIL-BAIT behavior — splits/bonuses at a LOW price 'for liquidity' (Captain: ₹10→₹2 split at a ₹100–190 price) and investor presentations formatted as broker research with 'buy' rationales; the AR that CONTRADICTS ITSELF (Captain FY21: 'no penalties by any authority' in one section, the BSE quorum fine admitted in another — the maker-checker isn't weak, the narrative is); INCOMPLETE ARs filed on exchanges (Tembo: exchange sought clarification for the missing consolidated auditor's report) and casino/gambling content appearing on the company's own website (Tembo — check the WEBSITE, not just the filings); and the promoter BLOCK-DEAL HARVEST at an honest company — the listco block-sells a 10% stake in the crown-jewel group co TO the chairman's wife at the previous close, saving the family weeks of market-impact cost that listco shareholders effectively donated (Triveni/Triveni Turbine FY23 — legal, disclosed, and still a transfer of opportunity).**
Note the nuance from the cases: management red flags are often a matter of *degree* — National Fittings/Kaveri/Ratnamani have strong businesses with *some* governance blemishes (not outright fraud), so the verdict is "get comfortable with management first," whereas Rexnord-style warrant abuse + backdoor control is a clean reject. Weight integrity breaches (self-dealing, fabrication) as vetoes; weight blemishes (one high salary, minor RPT) as cautions to price in.

---

## 8. MARGIN OF SAFETY — the cornerstone (Stage 7 GATE: need BOTH kinds)

MoS = the cushion for being wrong. A buy needs MoS in **price** AND in **business**:

**A) MoS in the PURCHASE PRICE (1 test):**
- **Earnings Yield (EY = EPS/CMP) > 10-yr G-Sec/Treasury yield.** Bigger gap = bigger cushion. → keep P/E < 10.

**B) MoS in the BUSINESS MODEL (2 tests):**
- **SSGR > achieved sales growth** (self-funded growth — §4). Higher = safer.
- **Positive Free Cash Flow, high FCF/CFO** (§5b). Higher FCF% = safer.
  - FCF = CFO − Capex. Capex = Δ(GFA+CWIP) over year, or Δ(NFA+CWIP)+Depreciation.
  - Logic: in a downturn, a high-FCF, high-SSGR firm can cut prices/dividends, extend customer credit, pay suppliers fast — *all without new debt*. (HUL: 10-yr CFO ₹26,298cr, capex only ₹3,718cr → **86% FCF**, dividends ₹20,543cr, debt-free → trades P/E ~40s. Atul Auto FCF% 48%, TTK 42%.) A 0%/negative-FCF firm (Bhushan Steel FCF% −300%; NFL negative) cracks at the first downturn.

**The rare jackpot = BOTH high MoS-in-business AND low P/E (cheap).** Most great-business names are already expensive (no price MoS); most cheap names are weak businesses (no business MoS). The whole game is finding the overlap. Only a handful in a lifetime are needed.

---

## 9. CREDIT RATING CHECK (Stage 8 GATE)

> Read **all** credit-rating reports (CRISIL/ICRA/CARE/India Ratings), latest *and historical*. Even free summary reports carry
> management-private info (customer names, contract terms, capacity utilization, expansion status, key risks) and a 3rd-party
> verification of the financials and of my own thesis.

- **Threshold:** current rating **≥ BBB-** AND **trend improving** over the years.
- **Direction > level:** prefer a co rated **BBB-→A-** (improving) over one **AA+→A** (deteriorating) even though the latter's absolute rating is higher. Rating *movement* is a clean proxy for fundamental trajectory.
- A *falling* rating while you think fundamentals are *improving* = recheck your analysis (you're likely missing something).
- Example arc: Oriental Carbon & Chemicals BBB (2008) → A (2015) tracked its real improvement (margins 12%→25%+, debt down), and reports explained the *why* (quarterly contracts to pass on raw-material costs; new customer approvals; value-added grades) that the annual report alone didn't reveal.

---

## 10. OPERATING-PERFORMANCE TREND (Stage 9 — confirm the engine, 5 steps over 10 yrs)

Confirms the business is *improving*, not decaying. Each should be **stable or improving**; multi-year deterioration = caution/avoid (and is also the SELL trigger §14).

1. **Sales growth** — consistent CAGR, no one-year spikes masking weak years.
2. **Profitability** — OPM & NPM stable/rising (not wild swings / steady decline).
3. **Operating efficiency:**
   - **Inventory Turnover = Sales/avg inventory** — stable/↑ (↓ = capital stuck / obsolete stock).
   - **Receivable Days = avg receivables/Sales ×365** — stable/↓ (↑ = can't collect → working-capital/interest drag).
   - **Fixed-Asset Turnover = Sales/Net Fixed Assets** — stable/↑ (↓ = capital-guzzling; e.g. Amtek's very low turnover drove its debt up 33×). FAT of 2 = each ₹1 of plant yields ₹2 sales.
4. **Profits → free cash** — cumulative **CFO should exceed cumulative PAT** (add-back of interest+depreciation should make CFO>PAT if working capital is managed). cCFO<cPAT = profit stuck in working capital.
5. **Free cash funds growth** — expansion funded by internal cash, **not spiraling debt.** (*Honda SIEL* = grew sales at the cost of profitability + falling inventory turnover + rising receivables + no free cash → avoid pattern.)

Note: compare a company to **its own** history (trend) rather than across industries — an infra co may run higher debt than a pharma co, but *rising* debt without matching sales/net-worth growth is bad in *both*.

---

## 11. THE FINAL CHECKLIST (consolidated PASS/FAIL — apply before any BUY)

> A stock should clear essentially all of these. Treat **Financial #8, all Management, Fraud flags, and Valuation price-gate** as
> non-negotiable. (No checklist is exhaustive — but anything failing here needs an explicit, strong reason to override.)

**FINANCIAL ANALYSIS**
- [ ] 1. Sales growth: **CAGR >15% for 7–10 yrs**, consistent (ignore 1-yr spikes; >50% = unsustainable)
- [ ] 2. Profitability: **NPM >8%**, sustained OPM & NPM
- [ ] 3. Tax payout: **≈ >30%** (near corp rate unless a stated incentive)
- [ ] 4. Interest coverage: **>3**
- [ ] 5. Debt/Equity: **< 0.5** (preferably 0)
- [ ] 6. Current ratio: **>1.25**
- [ ] 7. Cash flow: **CFO > 0** (great if CFO covers CFI+CFF)
- [ ] 8. **cPAT ≈ cCFO** over 10 yrs
- [ ] 9. **Value created per ₹1 retained ≥ 1** — ΔMcap(10y) ÷ retained earnings(10y); metric valid only on a clean solvent base (§2[V6], §5[V8])
- [ ] 10. **FCFE = FCF − interest > 0** (else dividends/growth are debt-funded, §2[V7])
- [ ] 11. **Not a low-NFAT × low-NPM debt trap** (kill-test #3, §2[V7])
- [ ] 12. **cCFO-vs-cPAT gap decomposed** and the cause identified as benign (other income / int+dep add-back / deliberate WC-moat) — not rising receivable-days (§2[V4]/[V7]/[V8])
- [ ] 13. **Any claimed pricing power proven by FALLING RM-%-of-sales** while OPM rises (§2[V8]); OPM change triaged: power vs efficiency vs luck (§2[V3])

**VALUATION**
- [ ] P/E: per the **4 Principles** target (base = 1/G-Sec, + moat/FCF/stable premiums); prefer **<10**
- [ ] PEG: **<1**
- [ ] EY: **> 10-yr G-Sec yield**
- [ ] P/B: **<1** (only weighted for financial sector)
- [ ] P/S: buy **<1.5**, sell **>3**
- [ ] Dividend Yield: **>0** (>5% attractive; ignore for fast-growers)
- [ ] **Cyclical → valued on MID-CYCLE/normalized earnings only**, never trailing peak (§2[V5], §5[V7])
- [ ] **Low-P/E trap check:** would it pass every gate at a *normal* P/E? If not, the cheapness is the market's correct verdict (§5[V5]–[V7])
- [ ] **High-P/E (≳40) de-rating scenario modeled** — growth must be durable enough to survive multiple compression (Just Dial 65→7 while PAT doubled, §5[V8])
- [ ] **One-offs stripped from E before any multiple:** DTL-reversal (§2[V7]), inventory/windfall gains, creditor write-backs (§25 Marksans), capital gains booked as income (§2[V6])

**BUSINESS & INDUSTRY (moat)**
- [ ] Sales growth **> peers**
- [ ] Production capacity & **volume** CAGR ≈ sales CAGR (volume-led, not price-led)
- [ ] **Profit CAGR ≈/≥ Sales CAGR**
- [ ] **cPAT ≈ cCFO** (profit collected as cash)
- [ ] **ΔMCap(10y) > retained profits(10y)** (creates, not destroys, value)
- [ ] Product: **pure play / related** (no unrelated diversification)
- [ ] **No govt interference** in pricing/profit
- [ ] **Not an anti-moat archetype:** contract-manufacturer-for-brands (NPM 1–3% cap), platform-disrupted incumbent, customer-captive supplier, temporary-windfall margin (§6[V6]/[V8], §25)
- [ ] **Not a sector veto:** EPC / PSU / milked-MNC-subsidiary (§6[V4], §7[V7])
- [ ] **No fatal concentration:** single supplier for a key input (Supreme–Kaneka §6[V8]), or top customers > ~50% of sales (Sonata top-10 = 70%)
- [ ] **Moat survives the founder leaving AND the key input/vendor changing** (AIA promoter-tethered moat, §6[V8]); succession real
- [ ] **Margins don't rest on a duty/subsidy/cartel/regulatory prop** that can be revoked (§6[V3]/[V4], Heidelberg cartel floor §24, Paushak license §24)

**MARGIN OF SAFETY**
- [ ] Price: **EY > 10-yr G-Sec**
- [ ] Business: **SSGR > achieved sales growth**
- [ ] Business: **FCF/CFO >> 0** (positive, high)
- [ ] **FCFE = FCF − interest > 0** (the levered-firm version of the same cushion, §2[V7])
- [ ] **No serial equity dilution** — recurring QIPs/preferential issues inside a "growth" story = not self-funding (Amber/IOL/Minda §25)

**MANAGEMENT — subjective**
- [ ] Background check: **nothing** questioning promoter/director integrity
- [ ] Succession plan in place; successor salaries modest/in-line

**MANAGEMENT — objective**
- [ ] Promoter salary: **no rise during declining profits/losses** (2–4% of PAT norm)
- [ ] Project execution: good, **no cost/time overruns** (exclude M&A)
- [ ] Dividend CAGR **>0** (rising with profits) **and funded by FCF (not debt)**
- [ ] Promoter shareholding **≈/>51%** (higher better)
- [ ] Promoter **buying** shares (insider buying = + ; follow them)
- [ ] **FII shareholding low** (undiscovered = re-rating upside)
- [ ] **No pledged** promoter shares
- [ ] No abusive **warrants**, no value-extracting **RPTs**
- [ ] **No listco-as-group-ATM:** no ICDs/loans to promoter entities, promoter-owned suppliers, sell-through-promoter firms, asymmetric promoter-JV terms (§7[V6])
- [ ] **No reverse-RPT:** not borrowing FROM promoters while cash-rich; no assets sold back to promoters (Atul §7[V8])
- [ ] **No conglomerate shuffle in either direction:** no junk/losses pushed IN (Just Dial/IOL §7[V8]), no good assets hived OUT without bidding (Century §7[V7]), no promoter-asset purchase promptly impaired (Minda AutoGas)
- [ ] **Auditor's report clean:** no qualifications, no un-booked MTM/forex/derivative losses (AIA/Amber/Marksans §7[V8]), no capitalised forex losses (Filatex §7[V7]), no reserve-routing of losses
- [ ] **Consolidation perimeter complete:** subsidiary list reconciles year-over-year; no entities out of scope at management discretion (Minda), none hidden (Marksans GmbH) (§7[V8])
- [ ] **Statutory hygiene:** taxes/PF/dues/CSR paid on time (chronic delays = integrity tell, §3[CS], §25)
- [ ] **Warrant-lapse check:** promoters didn't forfeit warrant money while the company defaulted on lenders (IOL §7[V8])
- [ ] **Density counter < 3:** total independent governance oddities across all checks — **≥3 = REJECT regardless of everything else** (§7[V8])

**CREDIT RATING**
- [ ] **≥ BBB-** and **improving** trend

**FRAUD / FORENSIC SWEEP (hard veto)**
- [ ] §3 toolkit run in full: cCFO-vs-cPAT (decomposed), DSO trend + receivables ageing, ITR, FCF-vs-CFO, serial-acquisition counter-metric, abnormal smoothness, accounting-policy changes
- [ ] **Cash-flow statement REBUILT to convention** — no borrowings/finance costs hiding in CFO or CFI, no investments inside CFO (NGL/Beekay/Jamna §2[V9]; Lloyds put borrowings inside CFO AND filed an MGT-9 showing zero debt §27); **CMLTD traced out of CFO's "other liabilities" inflows** (Ramco/Rushil kill-test #14); CFO recomputed clean; **FCFE computed** (kill-test #2 — Asahi/Laurus/Safari §2[V10])
- [ ] **Accounting-regime rebuilt (kill-test #14):** IndAS-115 pass-throughs grossed up before margin trends (Linde); cumulative exceptional gains vs cPAT computed (Linde 64%); write-back income stripped from "profits" (Websol); AR copy DIFFED against the exchange-filed copy of the same report (Linde/Websol §7[V11])
- [ ] **If a recent SME/small IPO: kill-test #13 structural screen passed** — issue size vs oversight thresholds, promoter-money-in-project, auditor churn, promoter background search, pledge trajectory, lock-in calendar (Varanium §28)
- [ ] **If the chart re-rated >5× in ≤2 years or the name carries a theme label: kill-test #15 story-stock screen passed** — shell lineage, announcement cadence vs independent verification, warrant/preferential timing vs the price run, promoter/insider selling into the rally, asset base vs story, filing quality (Nibe/RIR/Tembo/TechNvision §29; any TWO fingerprints = REJECT)
- [ ] **Revenue mix REBUILT from segment notes (kill-test #16)** — trading share and segment margins known; NFAT >10 investigated as a trading tell; the breakout year's CFO checked (negative-CFO growth = receivables, not commerce); SSGR validated against cPAT≈cCFO before being cited (Tembo 76% trading; KPI plot sales; Dynamic §2[V12])
- [ ] **Capex reconciled to capacity/revenue added** — no spent-but-invisible capex (SOM §7[V9])
- [ ] **Related-party list reconciled year-over-year vs the shareholding pattern** — no de-classified promoters (SOM SDPL), no vanished related parties (Valiant/Aarti), no undisclosed spouses (Lincoln)
- [ ] **Marketing/promotion % of sales and any "advances" benchmarked vs peers** — spend ≫ peers routed to counterparties = extraction suspect (SOM §7[V9])
- [ ] The balance-sheet combos: Rising Sales+Receivables+Debt; High-cash+High-debt; vanity-metric headlines (§3)
- [ ] **Template dashboard built (§16b)** — via the repo xlsx (`DrVijayMalik_Screener_Excel_Template_v3.2.xlsx`) or the on-the-fly recipe — verdict strip (FCFE.1/.2, value-per-₹-RE, SSGR, cPAT-vs-cCFO, RM%-trend) read against the kill-tests, fund-flow table added manually
- [ ] **Forensic catalog swept:** §18c→§29b, items **1–278** — any match investigated before proceeding

**PATTERN MATCH (do this FIRST and LAST)**
- [ ] Company matched to its nearest archetype (§17a + maps atop §18–§29) and nearest of the **140 cases** (§17b–§29a); that card's reasoning read
- [ ] My verdict is **consistent with the matched case's verdict** — if it differs, I have written down exactly which fact differs and why it flips the outcome

→ **All clear → BUY** (size per §12). **Any hard veto (fraud/management/price) → REJECT.**

---

## 12. BUY DECISION & PORTFOLIO CONSTRUCTION (Stage 10)

**Buy decision = passes §11 + price ≤ target P/E (§5b).** Then:

**Portfolio sizing rules:**
- **Number of stocks: between 2 and 30.** Min 2 (from different industries) for any diversification; beyond ~30 adds *no* further risk reduction and dilutes winners. **Keep the number as LOW as comfortable** (concentrate in best ideas — Buffett: "buy worthwhile amounts when convinced").
- Cap the count by **monitoring bandwidth**: each stock/yr ≈ 4 quarterly results + 4 shareholding disclosures + 1 annual report + 1 credit report + exchange filings + news alerts. (25 stocks ≈ 100 quarterlies, 25 annual reports, etc.) Hold only as many as can be monitored well.
- **New investor:** keep it *small*; park excess in index/MF until stock-picking skill is proven. (Large portfolio ≠ safety; it's harder to monitor.)

**Capital-allocation order (every time new cash arrives):**
1. **First, add to existing well-understood holdings** that still pass the test and are below target P/E (lowest research cost, known management).
2. **Sell** any holding that has **failed the test for 2 consecutive years** (purge laggards).
3. **Only if** nothing in the portfolio is investable → search for a NEW stock.

**Behavioral execution rules (these *are* decision rules):**
- After a clean buy, **price declines on intact fundamentals = BUY MORE** (groceries-on-sale). Recessions reliably recover. (JK Lakshmi fell ₹80→₹32, then IRR 115%; Allahabad Bank ₹70→₹37 then IRR 102%; Haldyn ₹16.5→₹10.3 then IRR 70% — author *added* on the dips.)
- **Hold through stagnation** if fundamentals intact (Mayur flat ₹100–120 for ~1 yr, then +400% in 10 months).
- **Don't chase** above target P/E — wait/hold rather than overpay; the market re-rates in unpredictable spurts.
- Trade as little as possible (cost + peace).
- Retail edge to exploit: monthly fresh savings (buy in bear markets when institutions can't), no redemption pressure, true long-horizon, can sit in cash when nothing is cheap. Use these.

---

## 13. MONITORING (after buying — keep the thesis alive)

Track **business/operating/management** parameters, **not** daily price. Cadence:
- **Ongoing:** Google Alerts for each holding + industry; read corporate announcements (capacity, M&A, mgmt changes, insider trades).
- **Quarterly:** results (filed within 45 days of Q-end, 60 days for Q4 — **a delay is a RED FLAG**, often fraud/governance, e.g. Ricoh India). Read P&L (YTD + quarter), segmental, B/S (in H1/FY), notes, **promoter shareholding & pledge** changes. Don't overreact to 1–2 soft quarters; *do* act if a permanent change appears.
- **Annual:** full annual report + refreshed credit rating (upgrade = +, downgrade = investigate).

Promoter **stake ↑** = positive (consider buying alongside); **stake ↓ consistently** or **new pledge** = investigate/concern. Track institutions only once they cross strategic 25%.

**[V9]–[V12] Monitoring additions (each of these caught a live deterioration in the case library — add to the cadence):**
- **After ANY transformative announcement by a holding** (mega order, new plant, acquisition, theme pivot): pull the **insider-trading disclosures for the following 12 months** — promoters/directors selling into the announcement rally is the exit signal itself (RIR ₹29cr; Varanium's lock-in-expiry dump). Also cross-check the announcement against the counterparty/govt record before crediting it (kill-test #15b applies to holdings too).
- **Warrant/preferential events:** any warrant allotment to promoters (note strike vs CMP, the 25/75 free-option asymmetry, lapse-and-reissue history) and any raise whose object is "working capital / general corporate purposes" — at a holding this is the WC-black-hole tell (Dynamic, Captain, Advait, Tembo §29).
- **Annually, diff the AR against the exchange-filed copies:** auditor observations vanished from the AR copy (Linde, Websol §7[V11]); AR sections missing entirely from the exchange filing (Tembo). Also check the **CARO/auditor note on bank stock statements** — a books-vs-bank divergence is inflation of the books (Tembo ₹28.57cr vs ₹44cr).
- **Track the subsidiary/step-down stake table YoY** (AOC-1): stakes quietly diluted (TechNvision 68.37→58.21%) or a WOS transferred to promoters (Tembo) = value exiting below the consolidation line.
- **Rating-agency CHURN, not just the rating:** a switch of agencies after an outlook cut is a concealed downgrade (Kanchi §22; Rushil's three agencies in months §28).
- **Resignations mid-run:** independent directors (especially marquee names — Vandrevala/Tembo), auditors, CFO/CS — treat a resignation during a price run or before results as a fire alarm, not HR news.
- **Royalty/brand-fee %-of-sales and the family's TOTAL take across group companies, annually** (PGHH's 1.8→5.4% ramp §7[V11]; Godfrey's 2%→6–7% of PAT §7[V10]; KPI's ₹20.4cr personal royalty §29).
- **Related-party schedule reconciled YoY against the shareholding pattern** — de-classified promoters and vanished related parties (SOM, Valiant §7[V9]) are how extraction goes dark.

---

## 14. SELL DECISION RULES (Stage 11)

> **Default = NEVER SELL A GOOD STOCK.** A 100-bagger passes through 2×, 10×, 50× first — selling early forfeits the wealth.
> **Sell decisions must be DISSOCIATED FROM PRICE** (ignore current price, buy price, and unrealized gain/loss).
> Do **NOT** sell on: doubling, target %, trailing stop-loss, "sell to recover cost." Those force you out of winners.

**SELL only when one of these *fundamental* triggers fires:**
1. **Deteriorating operating performance for ≥2 consecutive years** — any of: declining sales YoY; declining OPM/NPM; declining efficiency (falling FAT, falling inventory turnover, rising receivable days); continuously rising debt to fund operations. (Require **2 consecutive years** — ignore 1–2 soft *quarters*; temporary dips are times to *buy*, not sell.) If the business advantage has genuinely eroded → sell regardless of profit/loss.
2. **Government starts capping pricing/profitability** of the company (regulated returns → suboptimal). (Gujarat State Petronet, IGL — PNGRB capped profitability.)
3. **Permanent adverse change in business dynamics** (impact not temporary). (Haldyn Glass — PNGRB cut gas supply, forced 20% capacity cut.)
4. **Portfolio has too many stocks to monitor** → sell the weakest to get back to a manageable count.
5. **Position has become a trivially small % of portfolio** (ran up, never added) and isn't worth the monitoring burden — either pray for a dip to add meaningfully, or sell.

Also exit immediately (overrides "never sell") if a **§3 fraud flag** or **§7 management integrity breach** surfaces post-purchase (e.g. self-dealing RPT, fabricated cash, delayed results signaling fraud). Don't anchor to your cost.
**[V12] The same override fires when a HOLDING starts behaving like a story stock (kill-test #15 post-purchase):** an announcement machine spinning up, warrants to promoters timed to a price run, promoters/insiders selling into announcement-driven strength, a theme pivot via object-clause change, or filings turning incomplete/self-contradicting — any two = exit into the liquidity the story created, exactly when it feels most rewarding to stay (the RIR/Nibe/Varanium sequence viewed from the inside). A rising price on manufactured news is a SELL input, not a HOLD input.

Accept that sold stocks may rise afterward — short-term price ≠ fundamentals ("voting machine short-term, weighing machine long-term").

---

## 15. WORKED-EXAMPLE LIBRARY (fast pattern-matching reference)

**GOLD STANDARD PASSES (what a buy looks like):**
- **Vinati Organics:** sales 34% CAGR (volume-led, beats peers), profit 44% CAGR, NPM 7%→12%, cPAT≈cCFO, D/E<1, ₹7.13 MCap per ₹1 retained; bought at P/E 7.7 → IRR 143% (P/E expansion = 81% of it). *The archetype.*
- **Mayur Uniquoters:** bought P/E 6.6 → IRR 136% (P/E expansion 104% of return); held through a year of flat price then +400%.
- **FDC, Container Corp, VST Tillers, Tide Water Oil:** SSGR >> growth, debt-free (self-funded growth).
- **Atul Auto (FCF% 48%), TTK Prestige (FCF% 42%), HUL (FCF% 86%):** high free-cash, debt-free, dividend from FCF.
- **Ambika Cotton, Manappuram:** shareholder-friendly management (modest/declined salary; honored commitments).
- **Oriental Carbon:** credit rating BBB→A improving, real fundamental improvement.

**INSTRUCTIVE REJECTS (what to avoid, and why):**
- **Tata Steel:** sales 28% CAGR but profit 0% (NPM 23%→2%, losses), FCF −₹20,508cr, debt ₹3,377cr→₹80,701cr, dividends debt-funded, ₹0.81 value per ₹1 retained → no moat + bad capital allocation. *Sales growth ≠ value.*
- **Amtek/Castex, Glenmark, Pratibha, Jaiprakash Power, LT Foods:** SSGR << growth → debt spirals (some to bankruptcy/asset sales). *Growth beyond means.*
- **Bhushan Steel (FCF% −300%), National Fertilizers (neg CFO):** cash guzzlers, debt-funded → fragile.
- **Brooks Laboratories:** perfect financials (sales +23%, profit +36%, debt-free) BUT promoter SEBI-fraud history → **REJECT on management alone.**
- **ESS DEE Aluminium:** promoter salary 2%→12% of PAT as profits fell → later debt ₹500cr, losses, stock ₹750→₹90.
- **Gujarat Automotive Gears, Rexnord:** related-party self-dealing (loans to promoter co; commission/warrants to promoter family) → value siphoned from minorities.
- **DCB Bank:** management U-turned a sound long-term plan after a 30% price drop (ESOP-protective) → short-termist.
- **SCI:** 2% sales CAGR, MCap fell → no moat, dead money.
- **Honda SIEL:** growth at the cost of margins + efficiency + cash → avoid.

**Quick numeric anchors to recall:**
- Good NPM ≥ 8% (Vinati 12%); thin/falling NPM (Tata Steel →2%) = bad.
- Good FCF/CFO ≥ ~40% (Atul 48, TTK 42, HUL 86); negative (Bhushan −300) = bad.
- Good promoter salary 2–4% of PAT (Ambika); bad ≥10% / rising-as-profit-falls (ESS DEE 12%).
- Good value-creation ≥ ₹1 per ₹1 retained (Vinati ₹7.13); bad < ₹1 (Tata Steel ₹0.81).
- Good D/E < 0.5 (≈0 ideal); danger = debt rising every year.
- Buy P/E < 10; avoid P/E > 20–25 (no expansion room, contraction risk).
