ABBOTT INDIA (Jun-2024, P/E 48 — quality-no-MoS).** FY15–24: sales ₹2,289→5,849cr (11%), OPM 14→25%, cPAT 5,845 ≈ cCFO 5,829 ✓, capex ₹524cr → FCF 6,285 ✓✓, SSGR 29–248% ≫ growth, cash ₹2,134cr, NFAT 16–39 (two-thirds outsourced), recv ~20d; value-per-₹-RE 23.51 — the closest-to-genuine high score in the set. *Mechanics:* 24,000-company market, 26 brands per molecule, Duphaston monopoly→41 brands in TWO years (moat-evaporation velocity); NPPA/DPCO price caps (Betonin discontinued; Thyronorm/Brufen capped). *Flags:* multi-entity Abbott structure = allocation risk (the Linde SEBI precedent applies); ₹200cr loan to a group co + interest-free deposits; RPT limits breached, ratified after the fact; cash at 3% until an analyst asked (kill-test: lazy treasury); FEMA sectoral-cap breach denied then compounded; KMP-relative rent deals. *Verdict:* the best pure business of the volume — and still an AVOID at 48× under parent-allocation risk; the §5[V7] Honeywell lesson on an FMCG-pharma chassis.

**6. VARANIUM CLOUD (SEBI interim order May-10-2024 — the kill-test #13 source case).** SME IPO Sept-2022, ₹40cr net. SEBI found: **~60% of net IPO proceeds (₹24.07cr) siphoned within two quarters** — ₹18.43cr to "BM Traders" (a fruits-and-vegetables wholesaler with zero employees at a residential address), ₹1.5cr to the promoter's villa-rental co the day proceeds landed; the three "commissioned" data centres did NOT exist (NSE site visits: no containerised data centre; electricity consumption November-2023 = ZERO units); sales ₹4cr→₹923cr faked through "Amtelfone Incorporated" (Seychelles address shared with Panama-Papers shells, no registry entry, no bank receipts against ₹266cr of claimed collections), "Courage Clothing LLP" (nonexistent), a 2-month-old zero-employee US subsidiary contributing 99% of a quarter's revenue; expenses faked as AWS purchases with zero payments; ₹34cr of "background verification" against ₹1.75cr of total employee cost; documents withheld citing a GST audit that didn't exist; pump announcements (Pokemon apparel! jewellery! OTT! a ₹1,250cr preferential issue to buy a company whose owner absconded) drove the stock ₹30.5→₹393, and the promoter dumped his ENTIRE free stake the day lock-in expired (the "shareholding classification error": 63%→36%). Every flag was public beforehand: the promoter's 2009 cheating arrest, Malta scandal and Rolta episode were googleable; the ₹36.9cr arbitration award was omitted from "Litigation: Nil"; PAT ₹178cr vs CFO ₹8cr; three auditors in three years and an RHP audit disclaimer; pledge 0→64%; both raises sized under oversight thresholds; the auditor is now referred to NFRA. *Verdict:* the complete SME-fraud anatomy — run kill-test #13 before ANY financial analysis of a small listing; "there is nothing called a reformed promoter."

**7. SESHASAYEE PAPER (May-2023, P/E 4.6 AT the cycle peak — mid-cycle only).** FY14–23: NPM 2%↔19% (28-yr range 1–17%); cPAT 1,321 < cCFO 1,742 ✓; FCF 1,283; debt-free (returned a sanctioned loan mid-project); SSGR 11–16% > 9% ✓; value-per-₹-RE 1.35 — the market pays ~nothing for retained commodity-cycle earnings. *Structure:* 800 mills, 25.2 MTPA capacity vs 18.6 MTPA demand + zero-duty ASEAN imports priced 10% below; Indian wood $120/t vs $60 overseas (2t/t of paper); zero R&D (a govt institute does it); closures-then-shortage cycles with expansions announced at tops (its own MDP-IV, Apr-2023, ₹700cr — the tell); FY23's 26% OPM = post-Covid reopening + 50% realization jump. *Governance:* CCI cartel probe (its own employee's email exposed industry price meetings; Trident confessed; the wood-mill probe pending); cross-holdings (SPB↔Ponni 27.45%/14.02%, HEB, SPB-PC); bought promoter co EIEEL for ₹12cr+2cr against ₹18 LAKH net worth; ICDs to promoter entities written off; OPG Energy investment vanished from the AR without a sale disclosure; the FY2014 RPT list pruned then restored; zero year-end finished-goods claims (channel-stuffing check). *Verdict:* competent conservative cyclical wearing a promoter-web discount — value only mid-cycle; 4.6 at a peak is the §5[V9] inversion, not cheapness.

**8. RAMCO INDUSTRIES (Jul-2023, P/E 11.9 — AVOID; the discount is correct).** FY14–23: OPM 8–16% cyclical (FY21 reverse-migration peak), FY14 net loss; cPAT ₹1,706cr vs cCFO ₹824cr — benign once decomposed: ₹977cr (57%) is non-cash associate share (Ramco Cements/Systems/Rajapalayam; benign case #5, §2[V11]); SSGR 32–44% (inflated by the same associate profits); **value-per-₹-RE 0.67 — FAILS kill-test #1**: ~₹570cr of surplus went into group cross-holdings (market value ₹3,000cr the market refuses to pay for). *Mechanics:* fibre-cement pricing borrowed from the SUBSTITUTE (hikes possible only when GI-steel rose); asbestos = stroke-of-a-pen risk (imports only from Russia/Kazakhstan; Brazil/Canada banned; Sec-144 protests); Sri Lanka royalties ₹23cr trapped by repatriation controls; UltraTech converter deal fixed the starving clinker plant (fixed-fee family) — later sold to the promoter's Ramco Cements. *Governance:* the cross-holding stake-inflation worked example (₹1 controls ₹2; ICD round-trip via subsidiary SIL to buy group shares; buying promoters'/family shares for exit liquidity); the sole-selling-agent trust (1% of sales, ₹61cr/10yrs, trustees = chairman + MD, listco lends to it); guarantees for weaker group cos (rating agency counts them as debt) while borrowing ₹150cr for its OWN capex; CMLTD-inside-CFO ₹41.3cr (FY15); 28-year-old MD at ₹8.24cr with the AR admitting pay↔performance disconnect; fraudulent DEPB scrips; FEMA APR delays. *Verdict:* the listco is the group's treasury; 0.67 per ₹1 retained is the market grading that honestly — the "cheap" P/E is the discount, not the opportunity.

**9. RUSHIL DECOR (Jun-2023, P/E 10.6 at peak margins — REJECT on density).** FY14–23: sales ₹256→838cr (14%), OPM 10↔18% (FY23 peak already fading on import resurgence), FY15 loss; cPAT 217 < cCFO 416 ✓ but **FCF −176, FCFE −318**; SSGR 2–9% ≪ growth → ₹530cr raised since FY11 (debt 89→406 + IPO + preferentials + rights ×2); interest coverage 0.7–4.9; value-per-₹-RE 3.45 (tiny base). *Mechanics:* commodity wood panels (same German tech everywhere), MDF capacity-bunching price wars (FY19-20 utilization 40–55%), imports 10–15% cheaper, no formula pricing in contracts; Laos sourcing plan killed by Laos's own wood-export ban; Vizag MDF cost overrun ₹190cr on ₹341cr (+56%); PVC unit at 14–15% utilization, loss-making 5+ years. *Governance:* SEBI fine — IPO proceeds repaid an undisclosed bridge loan the RHP denied; rights issues at 30–40% discounts converting PROMOTER loans to equity with additional-subscription rights (stake 53.24→59.86%) while calling 0.85% debt too cheap to prepay; CMLTD-inside-CFO ₹21.5cr; rating-agency shopping (CARE→IndRa→3-notch cut→Infomerics); ID whose family held 9.55%; a 26-year-old CA-Inter ID; secretarial + internal auditors resigned in succession; dividends while FCFE-negative; corp office rented from the CMD (rent +129% in 3 years); promoter-owned brand charging royalty; wives as SVPs; son's minimum-20%-increment contract; loss-year bonuses; hacked-invoice fraud, cash theft, lost title deeds, EBITDA "growth" claimed while it fell; IT dept ATTACHED a unit; ₹190cr tax demand. *Verdict:* the SME governance zoo — density veto long before valuation; peak-cycle "10.6" is noise.

**10. WEBSOL ENERGY SYSTEM (Dec-2023, loss-making — REJECT; the treadmill archetype).** 28-yr cumulative PAT **−₹250cr**; FY23 sales ₹17cr→TTM ₹1cr (plant scrapped mid-rebuild); equity NEGATIVE FY14–16 (D/E −2.7); WC cycle 687 days; receivable days 305; value-per-₹-RE **−24.03** — negative RE with a POSITIVE theme-driven Mcap (₹14→345cr): the hope-premium artifact. *History:* technology scrapped three times (reclaimed → solar-grade → multi-crystalline → Mono-PERC/TOPCon 1,800MW rebuild); write-offs FY08/FY15(₹50cr+)/FY17(₹11cr)/FY22-23 (everything); two BIFR trips, NPA, two OTS rounds (₹188cr principal haircut to capital reserves), loans sold to ARCs which sold its properties, an 11-month default on a recalled loan; **profits were write-backs** — FY17 ₹71cr lender/FCCB waivers, FY21 ₹69cr sundry balances, FY13/18/19 more (the write-back-earnings test, kill-test #14c); FCCBs converted NINE years late under threat of director disqualification; solar bids collapsed ₹17.9→₹2.4/kWh; imports 25% cheaper; every JV died ≤2 years (Singapore ×2, Germany; Exide tie-up ended in a lawsuit + ₹30cr charge on its assets). *Governance:* pay +119% during the five-loss-year default stretch, ₹1.32cr in a zero-revenue year; granddaughter (26) as ED+CFO at ₹1cr; comfort letter for the promoter's WIFE's personal loan; fixed-asset records incomplete FY99→FY21 while scrapping assets; no internal audit until FY2012; auditors couldn't confirm debtor/creditor balances; the AR bragged "debt-free… competitive survivor… impressive" over a 94% production crash; secretarial-audit copy-paste errors 4 years running; exchange-copy vs AR-copy diffs. *Verdict:* a follower on a technology treadmill funded by other people's haircuts — REJECT; the 1,800MW rebuild is a policy lottery ticket, not a business record.

### 28b. Forensic red-flag catalog — Vol 11 additions (items 237–258)

237. **Ad-spend far above peer norms as a WEAKNESS marker:** 3–8× the category leaders' A&P-%-of-sales (Relaxo 8–12% vs Bata 2.5%) buys shelf presence, not pricing power; pair with the hike-tolerance test — a price rise that loses share proves the "brand" is rented (§2[V11]).
238. **The growth-or-margin throttle:** if history shows sales only grow when A&P surges and margins only recover when A&P is cut (PGHH's three regimes), the P&L has no compounding gear — model EITHER growth OR margin, never both.
239. **Mandatory maintenance capex disguised as growth capex:** design/mould churn (Relaxo ₹25–30cr/yr, half the range annually), certification cycles, environmental upgrades (Seshasayee) — subtract from FCF as a permanent cost before crediting "expansion."
240. **The promoter share ROUND-TRIP:** listco sells its stake in a promoter entity to the promoters cheap, then acquires the same entity dear by merger years later (Relaxo ₹4.32cr out → ₹14cr back; the full merger: ₹270cr in shares for ₹153cr of gross assets, saving ₹6.2cr rent) — check both legs of every promoter-entity transaction across years.
241. **Remuneration at the statutory ceiling to the LAST DECIMAL + family-slab equal pay** (Relaxo: limit ₹19.3137cr, paid ₹19.3137cr; brothers identical to the rupee; four cousins identical) — pay set by entitlement and bloodline is an extraction posture even when legal.
242. **IndAS-115 pass-through netting faking margin re-ratings** (Linde OPM 15→24% with profit unchanged; PGHH trade discounts ~6% of sales vanishing into net revenue) — gross-up across the FY2019 transition before ANY margin-trend conclusion (kill-test #14a).
243. **Exceptional-gains-as-the-business:** cumulative exceptional items vs cumulative PAT-before-exceptionals (Linde 64% over 37 years) — a chronically capital-heavy operator monetizing real estate/divisions every year is a liquidation annuity, price it as one (kill-test #14b).
244. **Shareholders vote a promoter RPT resolution DOWN (Linde 93.95%) and/or the company SUES the regulator to halt a probe (Linde's HC writ against SEBI)** — both are terminal governance verdicts by the two parties who know most; also watch failed delistings as intent (extends catalog 226).
245. **Exchange-filed report vs annual-report COPY diffs** — observations present in the version filed with the exchanges missing from the AR version of the same document, same date (Linde's RPT breach; Websol's noncompliance list) — diff them; the deletion is the finding.
246. **The acquisition-write-off treadmill in pharma/IP assets:** buying drugs/ANDAs/companies near their peak from sellers who know the cliff (DRL: Betapharm ~₹4,000cr, gNuvaring ₹1,113cr of ₹1,426cr, FY20–22 impairments ₹1,675/854/930cr, Nimbus >80% in a year) — treat the impairment RUN-RATE as a recurring cost when valuing serial acquirers.
247. **Growing aggressively INTO a collapsing jurisdiction** (DRL's Venezuela: 52% CAGR to #4 market while the currency imploded → ₹584cr written off; Ramco's Sri Lanka royalties trapped by the central bank) — country-risk discipline is a management-quality variable; repatriation controls convert profits into paper.
248. **Exclusivity-cliff mechanics:** day-181 price collapse brings chargebacks/refunds and inventory write-downs as a STRUCTURAL annual cost (DRL ₹900–1,000cr/yr) — normalize generic-pharma margins for the cliff, never on the exclusivity year.
249. **MNC brand/business ALLOCATION risk, regulator-validated:** parent runs multiple Indian entities under one management, migrates brands/plants/new launches to the unlisted arm (P&G: detergents, Pampers, the ₹2,000cr export hub; royalty 1.8→5.4% while listco R&D = nil), restricts the listco's territory/products (Linde — SEBI ordered an assessment of business forgone) — map every parent entity and the last five launches before paying for "the parent's pipeline."
250. **The listed MNC as group bank:** loans to affiliates (PGHH ₹2,200cr in one year, ₹1,911cr to the unlisted arm; Abbott ₹200cr + interest-free deposits), 15-year ICD programs to unnamed counterparties (PGHH FY96–FY10), RPT limits breached then ratified after the fact (Abbott ×3 in one half-year) — the cash is managed for the group's convenience, discount it.
251. **The lazy-treasury test:** cash yield far below the FD rate for years (Abbott ~3% on ₹2,500cr) until externally questioned — an auditable, repeatable management-quality metric; sub-FD yield on a large cash pile = unmanaged or purposed-for-the-group.
252. **SME-IPO threshold-dodging and no-skin structures** (Varanium: ₹40cr issue < ₹100cr monitoring threshold; ₹49.46cr rights < ₹50cr SEBI-review line; projects 100% IPO-funded, promoter contribution ₹15 lakh) — sizing chosen to avoid oversight is itself the disclosure (kill-test #13).
253. **Fake-counterparty fingerprints:** dominant customers with no registry existence, offshore addresses shared with shell directories, sales without bank receipts, a weeks-old zero-employee subsidiary contributing ~all revenue, expenses to a vendor with no payments in its ledger, purchase quantums absurd against employee count (₹34cr background checks / ₹1.75cr payroll), documents "with the GST department" (Varanium) — any one of these justifies walking.
254. **Pump-and-dump choreography:** unrelated-glamour announcements (Pokemon/jewellery/OTT), a giant preferential-issue acquisition target whose owner absconds, price up 10×, then the promoter sells his ENTIRE free stake at lock-in expiry disguised as a "classification error" in the shareholding pattern (63%→36%) — track the lock-in calendar and pledge trajectory (0→64%) as the fuse.
255. **CMLTD-inside-CFO (named trick, two confirmations):** current maturities of long-term debt routed through "other current/financial liabilities" so the increase lands in CFO while CFF shows only ex-CMLTD borrowings (Ramco ₹41.3cr FY15; Rushil ₹21.5cr FY21) — reconcile CFO's liability inflows to the borrowings notes every time (kill-test #14d).
256. **Rights issues as promoter-stake machines:** deep-discount rights whose stated object is repaying PROMOTER loans, with promoters reserving additional-subscription rights (Rushil ×2: stake +6.6pp) — debt-to-equity conversion at a discount is a warrant by another name; doubly so when management simultaneously calls its bank debt too cheap to prepay.
257. **Rating-agency shopping, second confirmation** (Rushil: outlook cut → new agency two notches higher within two months → 3-notch downgrade in 8 months → third agency): treat agency churn as the downgrade it conceals (extends §22 Kanchi).
258. **Write-back earnings and the survivor narrative:** profit histories built from creditors' losses — OTS interest waivers, principal haircuts to capital reserves, sundry balances written back (Websol FY13/17/18/19/21) — while ARs celebrate being "debt-free… a competitive survivor"; with negative equity years, 300-day receivables and a 687-day WC cycle, the ratios aren't conservative, they're post-mortem (kill-test #14c).

### 28c. Buy/sell decision reinforcement (all thirteen sources)

- **Vol 11's core: pedigree is not pricing power, and the cash register is not yours.** Five famous names failed the hike-tolerance or allocation test the market never ran — Relaxo (114×, customers fled a price rise), Linde (113× on an accounting artifact + a parent suing SEBI), PGHH (67× while brands migrate to the unlisted arm), Abbott (48× under allocation risk), DRL (2.05 per ₹ retained after the write-off treadmill). **Run the same gates on famous names as on micro-caps; fame just raises the price of the same weaknesses.**
- **The value-per-₹-RE spectrum is complete — classify, then conclude:** 216.80 (payout artifact) / 26.29 (re-rating) / 23.51 (genuine-ish) / 15.11-vs-P/E-114 (mispriced mediocrity) / 3.45 (tiny base) / 2.05 (acquisition drag) / 1.35 (cycle) / 0.67 (FAIL — group treasury) / −24.03 (hope on losses). One metric, nine readings — the classification IS the analysis.
- **The SME market has its own physics:** oversight thresholds dodged by design, IPO-funded no-skin projects, fake counterparties, pump announcements and lock-in-expiry dumps (Varanium); governance zoos with rights-issue stake machines and rating shopping (Rushil); technology treadmills funded by creditor haircuts (Websol). **Kill-test #13 runs BEFORE financial analysis down here — the numbers are the last thing to trust, and SEBI arrives after the money is gone.**
- **Accounting regimes are part of the data:** IndAS-115 netting, associate-profit consolidation, CMLTD placement, write-back income, exceptional-gain dependence — five mechanical rebuilds (kill-test #14) that flip apparent margin jumps, cPAT-cCFO gaps and "profits" into their true readings. Rebuild first; trend second.
- **Believe the numbers over the narrative (thirteenth confirmation).** "Impressive performance" (Websol — a 94% production crash), "no relationship between remuneration and performance" (Ramco's own AR — at least it was honest), "inadvertent error in classification of promoter shares" (Varanium — the dump itself), "current structure is delivering strong results" (P&G — for the parent it is). **The numbers overrule the words.**

---

## 29. COMPANY ANALYSES VOL. 12 — THE STORY-STOCK HARVEST & BULL-MARKET-PROMOTER-PLAYBOOK LIBRARY (fourteenth source)

> **The theme of the volume:** how promoters harvest a bull market, end to end — and how to catch it mechanically before SEBI does.
> Eight of ten names are SME/theme stories (defence, green energy, semiconductors, "AI software") whose charts re-rated 3–50×
> while NINE of ten printed negative FCFE; the two honest businesses (Dynamic Cables, Triveni) still failed on FCFE or price.
> The playbook's stations: **acquire a listed shell → switch the object clause to the season's theme → run the announcement machine →
> raise via warrants/preferentials timed to the run → extract via land/royalty/RPT/IP-purchases/goodwill → sell into the rally.**
> Kill-tests #15 (story-stock screen) and #16 (revenue-mix rebuild) come from this volume and run at STEP 0.

### 29a. Archetype map + the ten verdict cards

| Archetype | Vol 12 case | One-line verdict |
|---|---|---|
| Listed-shell defence story (announcement machine) | Nibe | REJECT — kill-test #15 on sight |
| Promoter-flywheel renewables developer | KPI Green | REJECT — FCFE −₹1,148cr; land+royalty extraction |
| Defence-pumps story on a textile-trading base | Tembo | REJECT — 76% trading revenue; books ≠ bank statements |
| Semiconductor-fab story + RPT playbook | RIR Power | REJECT — promoters sold ₹29cr into their own announcement rally |
| "Energy transition" diversification blitz | Advait | REJECT — ₹40cr of fresh equity into a stranger's bankruptcy |
| Fan contract manufacturer at story multiple | Wonder | REJECT — cCFO ₹2cr on cPAT ₹51cr at P/E 163 |
| Honest SME converter, WC-devoured | Dynamic Cables | AVOID — best business in the book, still FCFE-negative + promoter self-dealing |
| Subsidy-receivable agri model | Captain Polyplast | REJECT — receivables age in YEARS; cross-holdings; warrant games |
| 23-year-loss "software products" announcement machine | TechNvision | REJECT — P/E 326 on cumulative losses |
| Govt-administered integrated cyclical (real) | Triveni Engineering | WATCH, DON'T BUY at P/E 28 — the volume's only kill-test-#1 pass (2.92) |

**1. NIBE LTD (P/E 273→89 — REJECT; the complete shell-to-₹2,650cr playbook).** FY15–24: sales ₹11cr→collapse to ₹1.4–2.5cr (FY20–22)→₹105→₹282cr (LTM ₹366cr) — the "growth" begins the year the story does. Datasheet: 10-yr CFO **−₹9cr**, capex ₹154cr, FCF −₹163cr, **FCFE −₹175cr**; debt ₹1→73cr; SSGR −8% to −558%; receivables days printed 395–1,521 through the shell years. **Value-per-₹-RE 141.17 = the re-rating artifact at maximum** (RE ₹19cr vs ΔMcap ₹2,637cr — the metric is measuring the pump; §5[V12]). *Mechanics:* promoter family assembled 54.08L shares for **₹3.48cr total** (₹5–9.77/share, 2015–2020) — control of what became a ₹2,650cr Mcap for the price of a flat; object clause switched to defence; FY24 revenue split ₹161cr TRADED vs ₹43cr manufactured (57% trading — kill-test #16); the FY20 balance sheet shows gross block ₹21 LAKH and screener fixed-assets 0.00 for years — the "defence manufacturer" had no plant until the run (land additions ₹27cr FY23, AFTER the chart moved ₹40→₹2,000); warrants allotted ~₹500, exercised near ₹2,000 (the free option exercised in the money); RPT: factory-premises lease ASSIGNED to a KMP entity for ₹32cr; six BSE LODR fines FY21–24; subsidiary Nibe Technologies acquired Dec-2022 on ₹1L share capital printing ₹3.26cr PAT within months. *Verdict:* two kill-test-#15 fingerprints were visible by FY22 (shell lineage + announcement cadence); everything after was harvest.

**2. KPI GREEN ENERGY (P/E 57→33 — REJECT; the promoter-land flywheel with a royalty meter).** FY15–24: sales ₹6→1,024cr (LTM ₹1,327cr); OPM 54–68% in the early "solar" years — because **FY17 revenue was ₹23.5cr SALE OF PLOT out of ₹25.8cr total** (a land developer in a solar costume; kill-test #16c); OPM 33–35% at scale. 10-yr: CFO ₹240cr vs capex ₹1,185cr → FCF −₹945cr, **FCFE −₹1,148cr**; debt ₹0.7→1,036cr (D/E 1.2, interest ₹103cr LTM); **CFO NEGATIVE in the growth years themselves** (FY21 −₹27cr; FY24 −₹57cr on +59% sales); receivables 30→102–141 days; Mcap ₹127cr (FY20)→₹10,172cr. Solar tariffs ₹12.12→₹2.44/unit and modules $0.64→$0.19/W = structurally deflating realizations (§6[V12]). *Extraction:* land advances TO the CMD Faruk Patel ₹50.26cr in FY24 (plus family members; plus a FY21-22 ₹2.66cr advance given-and-returned round trip); **royalty to the CMD personally ₹20.42cr in FY24** for the "KP" name; deposits from four group SPVs; NEGATIVE sundry-creditor balances for PLOT/LAND in payables (netting chaos); the investor deck swaps IPP and CPP MW between pages (313 IPP + 132 CPP on one page; 158 IPP + 287 CPP on another — same 445 total); CMLTD parked in Other Current Liabilities (PFC ₹7.98cr appearing from zero — kill-test #14d); a Q1-FY25 filing letterheaded "August 8, 2025" (a year in the future). CPP = 87.9% of H1-FY25 revenue (captive customers, not the "IPP platform" story). *Verdict:* MW growth is real, cash is not; the family collects on land, royalty, and warrants whatever the equity does.

**3. TEMBO GLOBAL INDUSTRIES (P/E 20–80 — REJECT; a defence story wearing a textile-trader's books).** FY15–24: sales ₹37→432cr (LTM ₹529cr) but **FY24 segment split: trading of fabrics/textiles ₹334cr (76%) at 2% margin vs manufacturing ₹105cr (24%) at 15%** — kill-test #16a's type specimen. 10-yr CFO **−₹7cr** vs cPAT ₹33cr; FCF −₹48cr, **FCFE −₹72cr**; FY24 alone: receivables +₹110cr, CFO −₹149cr in the breakout year; WC cycle 57–158 days; debt ₹7→50cr; Mcap ₹70→810cr. *Governance:* the WOS carve-out (subsidiary formed inside the listco, taken over personally by promoters as the defence theme monetized — §7[V12]); **bank-stock-statement vs books: Q4-FY24 ₹28.57cr to the bank vs ₹44.00cr in books (54% gap)** "figures were on estimate and inclusive of tax"; consolidated auditor's report NOT filed with results (NSE clarification); NSE fine ₹70,800 (Reg 23(9) RPT disclosures); FY19 P&L classified ₹57.2cr of a ₹61.6cr income year as "Administrative and Finance Cost" (expense-line chaos); celebrity independent director Firdose Vandrevala resigned by HANDWRITTEN letter ("busy schedule and preoccupation with other matters") as the stock ran ₹200→₹750; rights + preferential raises whose objects were working capital and loan repayment. *Verdict:* the story is pumps-for-defence; the books are a fabric trader financing receivables with equity.

**4. RIR POWER ELECTRONICS (P/E 228→87 — REJECT; the Mehta RPT playbook + selling into the fab announcement).** 29-year record: NPM −14% to +6% pre-2006, barely profitable through 2015; FY15–24 sales ₹27→67cr (LTM ₹74cr), OPM 9–15%; CFO ₹19cr, FCF −₹15cr, **FCFE −₹26cr**; receivables 98–165 days; **value-per-₹-RE 97.04 = re-rating artifact #2** (RE ₹21cr vs ΔMcap ₹2,083cr on a semiconductor-fab story; Mcap ₹16→611→1,715cr). *The playbook (§7[V12]):* promoter entity sold the listco a DEFECTIVE plant; the entity went bankrupt and the promoters took it back; then Sicamore sold the listco **₹42cr of UNDEVELOPED IP** priced by a replacement-cost report (dev cost $1.47M × 110% overhead markup × 20% "developer's profit" × 19.5% required return × 3-yr lead = "$4.8M fair value" — arithmetic dressed as a fairness opinion); an earlier business transfer paid ₹210L for ₹136L of net assets (goodwill ₹74L). *The harvest:* since the Odisha-fab announcement (Nov-2023, stock ₹727), **the Mehta family sold 2,06,974 shares for ₹29.02cr at ₹835–2,587** (Bhavna ₹21.98cr; Ila ₹5.26cr; Jitendra ₹1.42cr; Naina ₹0.35cr) — and **independent director R.G. Trasi sold ALL 7,000 of his shares (₹89.5L)**; deferred-tax credits manufactured PAT twice (₹10.7L credit on ₹25.6L PBT; ₹36.1L on ₹1.67cr); 12.5% interest + rent to the promoter on his unsecured loan; the "₹24,000cr RRP semiconductor investment" tweet (1M views) sat alongside Tata/Micron in the hype list. *Verdict:* watch what the family DID while the announcement ran — they left.

**5. ADVAIT ENERGY TRANSITIONS (P/E 71 — REJECT; the diversification blitz that wired fresh equity into a stranger's bankruptcy).** FY15–24: sales ₹11→209cr (39% CAGR, LTM ₹238cr); OPM losses→17%; competitive-bidding L1 space (power T&D products + EPC; 10% retention held 2 years). cPAT ₹49cr vs cCFO ₹23cr (FY18–24); FCF −₹31cr, **FCFE −₹45cr**; SSGR 20–30% vs 39% growth → debt ₹2→59cr + IPO ₹6.88cr (issue costs 10.2%! 75% of net for WC) + **preferential ₹82.3cr at ₹1,388 (Jul-2024) + warrants ₹25.5cr (Sep-2024)** + WOS stake sold down 100→76.32%. *The blitz:* green-hydrogen electrolysers, fuel cells, solar EPC, carbon credits, IT/IoT — tie-ups everywhere, and **₹40cr of the fresh ₹82.3cr raise into listed shares of Norway's TECO 2030 (4.89%, Sep-2024) → TECO filed bankruptcy Dec-10-2024**, trading suspended, equity claim last in line — plus Yes Bank shares on the side, while paying a ₹3cr dividend on negative FCF (a pass-through of the raise). JV TG Advait (33.5% listco + promoters PERSONALLY + Chinese partner): ₹10.8cr in, ₹18.5cr accumulated losses, auditor going-concern doubt, ₹25cr listco guarantee — and the listco BUYS from the JV (personal-stake conflict disclosed in its own RHP). Buys/sells goods with the founder's father's proprietorship (figures differ between consecutive ARs: ₹25cr vs ₹21.4cr for the same year). Q2-FY25 PAT ₹4.77cr > PBT ₹3.67cr via a ₹2.88cr deferred-tax credit. BSE fine ₹4.6L (board composition); the warrants announcement filed with wrong EGM/BSE dates, wrong warrant count (86.9L vs actual 1.42L), wrong money received — refiled next day; named ITSELF as its new subsidiary in the AR. *Verdict:* announcement machine + capital misallocation at announcement speed.

**6. WONDER ELECTRICALS (P/E 163 — REJECT; the fan-OEM anti-moat priced as a story).** FY15–24: sales ₹72→570cr (26% CAGR, LTM ₹684cr); OPM ~4%, NPM ~2%, **zero economies of scale** (sales doubled FY18→LTM, margins flat); OEM conversion-cost model for Usha/Luminous/Surya (customers set RM specs, keep multiple suppliers, pay in 121 days while giving none); no patents, no brand, no collaborations (RHP admits all three); ODM = better margin but RM risk. **cPAT ₹51cr vs cCFO ₹2cr** — receivables consumed ₹204cr (₹17→221cr); FCF −₹72cr (capex ₹73cr), **FCFE −₹93cr**; debt ₹17→85cr (D/E printed −9.4 in the negative-equity FY15); SSGR 4–5% post-Hyderabad vs 26% growth; the Hyderabad plant's project cost was 53% WORKING CAPITAL (₹16 of ₹30cr); exports "focus" → forex earnings NIL. *Governance:* acquired promoter-owned Uttaranchal Industries for **₹23.42cr in 5% NCRPS against ₹8.24cr of net assets → ₹15.18cr goodwill paid to the promoters for a business they already ran**; buys ₹54.7cr/yr from Stamping & More LLP + ₹15.2cr Quality Components (promoter group entities in the SAME line, RHP-disclosed conflict); shareholder-approved managerial pay cap raised 11%→**40% of net profits**. Mcap ₹60cr (FY21)→₹1,821cr. *Verdict:* Amber's anti-moat (§6[V8]) with SME governance, at triple Amber's multiple.

**7. DYNAMIC CABLES (P/E 27 — AVOID; the volume's most honest business, still uninvestable).** FY15–24: sales ₹260→768cr (13%, LTM ₹935cr); OPM 4–11%→10%; NPM 1→5–6%. GENUINE improvements: govt:private mix inverted 80:20→20:80; HT-cable share 34.9→57.8% (conductors 20.6→8.6%); railways ~4%; solar cables; US sales team; RM bought only against orders; credit insurance on receivables; capex from internal accruals; no execution delays (India Ratings); 85% utilization; next ₹35cr capex ≈ +₹100cr revenue. **The SSGR lesson (kill-test #16d): SSGR ~25% > growth 13%, yet debt ₹49→119cr (nearly all WC), IPO ₹22.6cr (₹21.4cr for WC), preferential ₹96.6cr @ ₹436 (Jun-2024, object: working capital)** — receivables ate ₹194cr, inventory ₹100cr; cPAT ₹174cr vs cCFO ₹136cr; FCF +₹27cr but interest ₹124cr → **FCFE −₹97cr**; receivables 91→110 days (peak 148; SEB/CEB payers; ₹1.74cr IBC claim). *Governance (the discount's reason):* corporate guarantee + company property mortgaged for promoter co MEIPL's loans; MEIPL operates FROM the listco's premises; buy/sell DG-sets/cables/scrap with it; **revolving promoter loans at 16.85%** (in ₹11.92cr / out ₹10.32cr in FY24 alone, every year); BSE fines ~₹10L for board composition (the promoter's wife resigned to cure it) + late RPT filing + late FY18 results; dividend not banked in 5 days; AR errors (indebtedness table start=end despite +₹41.6cr; number-vs-words mismatches; two simultaneous "Head of HR"s). *Verdict:* the cheapest and most real name in the cohort — and still FCFE-negative with a family toll-booth attached; the correct price for it is lower than the market's.

**8. CAPTAIN POLYPLAST (P/E 26 — REJECT; the subsidy-receivable model + every promoter trick in miniature).** FY15–24: sales ₹99→294cr (13%, LTM declining ₹277cr); OPM 8–15% volatile; crude-linked granules from IOCL/RIL (zero supplier power), govt caps MIS prices (deviation 5–10%), subsidy = 50–70% of ticket paid by govt "in 4–6 months" — in practice **51% of receivables >1 year (₹55cr), ₹14cr >3 years** (AP nodal agency alone ₹35cr); +5% retention; del-credere IOCL agency (loss-making FY21); March inventory peaks; receivables days 122→217→182; CARE downgraded to BB+ (non-investment) Dec-2019; election quarters pause orders and GOOD monsoons hurt demand. cPAT ₹72cr vs cCFO ₹25cr; FCF −₹2cr, **FCFE −₹86cr** (interest ₹84cr); debt ₹22→103cr; **value-per-₹-RE 5.89 — a numeric pass that the rest of the card vetoes** (and NFAT 20.4 = trading tell, SSGR ~30% void — kill-test #16b/d). *Governance:* **mutual cross-holdings** (CPPL 17.5% of Captain Pipes ← → Captain Pipes 13.76% of CPPL — vote inflation with the public's money; the ASSOCIATE even subscribed the listco's warrants); serial warrants FY24 + FY25 (48L @ ₹72 = ₹34.56cr "for WC"; the 25/75 free option — §7[V12]); promoter pledge 15.2% (watch the pledge→warrant loop); buys ₹21.6–31cr/yr from Captain Pipes; 12% promoter-loan interest; pay exceeded LEGAL limits FY09–12 (RHP admission) and took 40% of PAT in FY22; built a new plant while the old one ran 40–50% on two shifts; bought listed stocks and MFs while indebted; ran windmills OUTSIDE its MoA objects; accepted banned notes in demonetization; insider-trading-reg breach FY23; board-quorum fines twice; **the FY21 AR says "no penalties" in one section and admits the BSE fine in another**; warrants reconciliation ADDED exercised warrants (75L balance vs actual 25L) while page 44 of the same AR says "no outstanding warrants"; ₹10→₹2 split at a ₹100–190 price "for liquidity"; investor decks formatted as broker research. *Verdict:* a working example of nearly the entire §7 veto list in one small-cap.

**9. TECHNVISION VENTURES (P/E 326 — REJECT; the announcement machine with a 23-year cumulative loss).** Lineage: listed shell Ankur Agencies taken over FY2000 → Solix Technologies → TechNvision (FY12). FY03–9M-FY25 cumulative: **net LOSS ₹6.4cr on ₹1,387cr of sales**; 9 years of zero growth FY05–13; defaulted on its Technology Development Board loan (secured by 17.5L shares of the HOLDING co) → one-time settlement = de-facto bankruptcy FY14–16; FY19 **wrote off ₹41cr of capitalized product development ≈ 4× all profits ever earned** — and the PPE note shows software disposals of ₹39.8cr gross vs ₹41.0cr accumulated depreciation = a NEGATIVE net carrying amount (botched even in the write-off); FY24 consolidated BS: **goodwill NIL, intangibles NIL, under-development NIL** — a "products company" (Solix "Gartner Magic Quadrant Leader alongside IBM/Informatica/HP", Emagia "IDC Major Player") with no products on the balance sheet and ₹0.38cr of software. 10-yr: PAT −₹12cr, CFO +₹3cr (positive only via the write-off add-back), **FCFE −₹13cr**; equity NEGATIVE FY21–23; receivables 276→97 days; **payables-as-unpaid-salaries** (₹27.6→52.8cr while only employee cost grew ₹59→90cr — §2[V12]); 99.97% overseas revenue, FY18: ₹43.6cr of ₹50cr consolidated revenue from UNAUDITED subsidiaries. *Structure:* gave the 53.67% HOLDING co Tiebeam ₹23cr (₹15cr "property advance" + ₹8cr loan) by TAKING a ₹14cr loan — returned within months (free bridge finance; "the Company is expected to render support… group company obligations"); step-down stakes diluted 68.37→58.21% (Solix) / 63.15% (Emagia) with the outside owners UNDISCLOSED; ₹15cr of "liabilities to Directors & Relatives" on the BS but absent from the RPT schedule; directors'-report income ₹124.7cr vs financials ₹75.9cr in the SAME AR (FY20); unexplained "Other liabilities" ₹101cr and Other Current Assets ₹28→65cr in six months. Mcap ₹14→996→3,178cr; **value-per-₹-RE −255.13** (the hope-on-losses artifact at scale). *Verdict:* every element of kill-test #15 in one name — the P/E is a rumor.

**10. TRIVENI ENGINEERING & INDUSTRIES (P/E 28.4 — WATCH, DON'T BUY; the only genuine business, capped by its regulator).** Sugar (7 UP mills, 10,000→63,000 TCD) + 860-KLPD multi-feed distilleries + IMIL/IMFL + high-speed gears (>80% share) + water treatment. FY15–24: sales ₹2,061→5,220cr (11%); OPM 0–18% (4 straight loss years FY12–16; FY23 NPM 32% only via the ₹1,496cr Triveni Turbine stake-sale gain); 29-year view: the record crush of FY17 came EXACTLY a decade after FY07's — cyclicality suppresses for ten years at a time (§6[V12]). **The only kill-test-#1 pass of the volume: value-per-₹-RE 2.92 on a real ₹3,300cr RE base**; receivables 26 days; debt FLAT ₹1,472→1,424cr across the decade; FCF +₹479cr; FCFE +₹1,531cr including the one-off (≈breakeven ex-gain); **dividends ₹370cr + buybacks ₹965cr (2020/21/23)** — a genuine capital returner. Inventory write-downs are the cycle's tax: FY18 ₹219.7cr, FY15 ₹111cr, FY19 ₹70cr, FY14 ₹55cr; FY24 inventory +21% on quota cuts. The administered chain (§6[V12]): SAP/FRP cane, monthly quotas, levy history, buffer stocks, export/import switches, ethanol feedstock caps (Dec-2023 surprise), molasses reservation (IMIL entered PRECISELY to escape levy-molasses), UPERC −40% tariff cut, the scrapped 2004 incentive policy (₹1,000cr invested; HC won after a decade; SC pending; ₹41.6+58.8cr contingent). *Governance blemishes to price:* the **promoter block-deal harvest** — FY23 the listco sold 10% of Triveni Turbine (3.23cr shares) to the chairman's wife at ₹229 ≈ the previous close via block window, sparing the family weeks of market-impact cost on a 14L-share/day float (§7[V12]); rent ₹2.19cr/yr to promoters; FY11 pay exceeded legal limits in an inadequate-profit year; auditor-flagged profit inflation in FY02 (WC interest excluded from inventory cost + ₹24.4cr pulled from GENERAL RESERVE into P&L) and FY99 (deferred rig idle-time costs); cheque frauds; UPPCB fine; a levy dispute from 1973-74 settled in 2007. Succession clean (group split between the two sons). *Verdict:* a real, disciplined, integrated survivor — but the state owns the P&L's biggest lever and P/E 28.4 on a cyclical at mid-history is no margin of safety. Re-examine at a cycle trough on mid-cycle earnings (§2[V5]).

### 29b. Forensic catalog additions (items 259–278) — sweep these on EVERY analysis

259. **Listed-shell acquisition + object-clause switch + serial rename:** control of a moribund listco assembled for pennies, objects switched by EGM to the season's theme, name changed to match (Nibe ₹3.48cr → ₹2,650cr Mcap; Ankur Agencies → Solix → TechNvision). Check MCA/AR history for every SME story stock: what WAS this company five years ago? (kill-test #15a)
260. **Announcement machine cadence:** MoUs, foreign tie-ups, badges ("Gartner Visionary", "Top AI Executives"), and even conference-registration openings filed as EXCHANGE announcements while cumulative profits ≈ 0 (TechNvision; Advait's tie-up blitz; Urja/Zacobite the SEBI-proven fake — the mineral did not exist). Verify EVERY material announcement against the counterparty or government record before crediting it (kill-test #15b).
261. **Warrants/preferentials timed to the price run:** allotment at the base, exercise near the top (Nibe ~₹500→~₹2,000); preferential at the high (Advait ₹1,388); serial issues after lapses at ever-lower strikes (Captain). The 25/75 warrant is a FREE promoter option — genuine infusion = 100% upfront at market (kill-test #15c; extends §7[CS]/[V10] Safari).
262. **Promoters/insiders selling into the announcement rally:** family selling ₹29cr at ₹835–2,587 in the months after the fab news (RIR); the INDEPENDENT DIRECTOR liquidating 100% of his holding (Trasi); a marquee ID resigning by handwritten "busy schedule" letter mid-run (Tembo/Vandrevala). Pull the insider-trading disclosures for the 12 months after any transformative announcement (kill-test #15d).
263. **Asset base vs story mismatch:** "defence manufacturer" with ₹21 lakh gross block and fixed assets = 0.00 on screener for years, land bought only AFTER the run (Nibe); "solar developer" whose first revenues were SALE OF PLOT (KPI). Read the PPE note's gross-block history against the narrative timeline (kill-test #15e).
264. **Majority-trading revenue behind a manufacturing label:** segment notes showing 57–76% traded goods at 1–2% margin under a defence/engineering story (Tembo 76%; Nibe ₹161cr of ₹282cr) — value it as a trader (kill-test #16a).
265. **NFAT >10–20 as a trading tell:** pure manufacturers run NFAT ≤5–6; a "manufacturer" printing 15–20 is substantially a trader/importer and its SSGR is void (Captain 20.4 with a loss-making del-credere agency) (kill-test #16b).
266. **Negative-CFO breakout years:** CFO negative in exactly the years sales exploded (KPI FY21/FY24; Tembo FY24 CFO −₹149cr on the record year) — the growth is a receivables entry until collected.
267. **Payables-as-unpaid-salaries:** trade payables doubling at a company whose only large, growing cost is employees (TechNvision ₹27.6→52.8cr vs wages ₹59→90cr) — a services firm has no goods creditors at that scale; suspect overdue wages = pre-insolvency.
268. **Deferred-tax-credit PAT manufacture:** DT credits exceeding current tax, printing PAT > PBT (Advait Q2-FY25; RIR twice) — recompute growth on PBT.
269. **Bank-statement-vs-books divergence:** quarterly stock/receivable statements to WC lenders materially BELOW the books (Tembo Q4: ₹28.57cr vs ₹44cr, "estimates and inclusive of tax") — the books are the inflated copy; check the CARO/auditor note on stock statements every time.
270. **Replacement-cost "IP" purchases from promoter entities:** undeveloped IP bought for ₹42cr on a man-months × overhead-markup × developer's-profit × required-return arithmetic (RIR/Sicamore) — a valuation REPORT is not a market price; any promoter-side intangible purchase = the Mehta playbook until proven otherwise.
271. **Promoter goodwill buyout:** the listco pays promoters for "goodwill" of a business the SAME promoters run inside the listco (Wonder: ₹23.42cr for ₹8.24cr of net assets, ₹15.18cr goodwill in preference shares) — what intangible did the listco not already own?
272. **Promoter-land flywheel + personal royalty rent:** project land routed through promoter-family purchases (₹50cr+ advances to the CMD, round-trip advances) plus a personal ROYALTY on the company's own name (KPI ₹20.42cr/yr) — the promoter collects on capex AND revenue regardless of shareholder outcomes.
273. **WOS carve-out & step-down stake leak:** a subsidiary created in the listco taken over personally by promoters as the theme monetizes (Tembo); listco stakes in the real operating step-downs quietly diluted with undisclosed counterparties (TechNvision Solix 68.37→58.21%) — the value exits BELOW the consolidation line.
274. **Listco as the holding company's bridge financier:** borrowing to lend upward as "property advances"/loans returned months later ("expected to render support… group obligations" — TechNvision/Tiebeam ₹23cr on a fresh ₹14cr loan); related-party liabilities on the BS but missing from the RPT schedule.
275. **Mutual cross-holdings between listed group companies:** each holds 13–17% of the other (Captain Polyplast ↔ Captain Pipes) — promoters control both blocks having paid for half; the associate subscribing the listco's warrants closes the loop (extends §7[V11] Ramco one-way math to the two-way case).
276. **Retail-bait behavior:** share splits/bonuses at LOW absolute prices "for liquidity" (₹10→₹2 at a ₹100–190 price — Captain); investor presentations formatted as broker research with "buy" rationales; PR-wire badges. Special effort aimed at retail behavior instead of the business = escalate diligence.
277. **The self-contradicting AR:** "no penalties by any authority" in one section while another admits the BSE fine (Captain FY21); two different resignation dates for the same officer (TechNvision); directors'-report revenue ≠ financial-statements revenue in the same AR (₹124.7cr vs ₹75.9cr) — when a document argues with itself, neither half is reliable.
278. **The promoter block-deal harvest (honest-company edition):** the listco block-sells a large stake in the crown-jewel group company TO the promoter family at the prior close, sparing them weeks of market-impact cost on a thin float (Triveni/Triveni Turbine: 10% to the chairman's wife at ₹229) — legal, disclosed, and still a transfer of opportunity from the listco's shareholders; log it as a governance debit at ANY quality tier.

### 29c. Buy/sell decision reinforcement (all fourteen sources)

- **Vol 12's core: in a theme bull market, the SME segment becomes a promoter-harvest machine — and the standard metrics INVERT.** The two most manipulated names printed the library's highest value-per-₹-RE scores (Nibe 141.17, RIR 97.04); P/E ranked the stories, not the businesses; SSGR passed at companies drowning in working capital. **The metrics that do NOT invert: FCFE (nine of ten negative), the breakout year's CFO, the segment-note revenue mix, and the insider-trading disclosures. In story territory, trust only the cash and the selling.**
- **Kill-tests #15 and #16 run BEFORE the financial gates, like #13:** shell lineage, announcement cadence, warrant timing, insider selling, asset-vs-story, filing quality — any two = REJECT; then rebuild the revenue mix before accepting any label. The Varanium lesson generalizes: **SEBI's order arrives after the money is gone; the fingerprints are public YEARS earlier.**
- **The honest names in a froth cohort still failed the buy test:** Dynamic Cables (real execution, FCFE −₹97cr, 16.85% promoter toll-booth) and Triveni (kill-test-#1 pass at 2.92, but a govt-administered P&L at P/E 28.4). **A bull market prices the honest and the fraudulent off the same theme; the discipline is refusing both — one on price, one on sight.**
- **Working capital is where SME profits go to die:** cPAT-vs-cCFO gaps of 25:1 (Wonder ₹51cr:₹2cr), receivables aging in YEARS on government subsidy chains (Captain), retention money held 2 years (Advait), receivables +₹110cr in a single breakout year (Tembo). **The P&L is an opinion; the receivables ageing note is the fact.**
- **Believe the numbers over the narrative (fourteenth confirmation).** "Defence manufacturer" (Nibe — a fan-trading shell with ₹21 lakh of gross block), "Powering Ahead" (Tembo — 76% fabric trading), "solar platform" (KPI — plot sales and a royalty meter), "Gartner-recognized product leader" (TechNvision — zero intangibles, 23-year loss), "100% pass-through" (Dynamic — margins still swing with copper), "energy transition" (Advait — ₹40cr into a stranger's bankruptcy in 3 months). **The numbers overrule the words.**
